# Rebuild Curation

A temporary, curatable file for the claudetto rebuild. Tick a box to accept an item; strike it out or delete it to reject it. Each "keep" is an idea, rule, algorithm, or behaviour, not a file. Sources:

- the deleted `openspec/`, read from `git archive 088a227c^ openspec`;
- the dossier `external/please-use-me-often/`;
- the Reddit exports in `external/<date>/`;
- the current tree at `088a227c`.

Each claim carries its basis: measured, cited, reported, or inferred.

## What We Have (088a227c)

- [ ] `plugins/claudetto/hooks/hooks.json`: `SessionStart` runs renice and caps build jobs and memory (`CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`, `GOMAXPROCS`, `GOMEMLIMIT`), then runs `scripts/session-start.ts`. `UserPromptSubmit` blocks `/claudetto:setup` when the plugin code is not loaded. `PreCompact` runs `scripts/pre-compact.ts`.
- [ ] `hooks/register.ts`: a mod that chains features through `kernel/chain.ts`. The price step is registered outermost, so it bounds what every later hook adds.
- [ ] `kernel/`: `chain`, `decider` (with `jev`), `shell/` (`shell-words`, `program-at`, `readonly`, `verdict`, `shell-state`), `secrets`, `settings`, `tools`, `note`, `heartbeat`, `absent`, `texts`.
- [ ] Features (folder: events, purpose):
  - [ ] `agent-model` (`agent.spawn`): sets the model from the agent file.
  - [ ] `budget` (`prompt.submit`, `session.messages`): context and weekly budget notes.
  - [ ] `compaction` (`session.compact`, `model.fork`, `turn.complete`): its own handoff in place of the core summary.
  - [ ] `decide` (`model.complete`, `http.fetch`): an MCP `decide` tool that asks a model for a choice; questions are keyed by index.
  - [ ] `delegation` (`agent.offer`, `tool.describe`, `tool.call`, `tool.register`): agent routing texts and caps.
  - [ ] `hygiene` (`settings.read`, `env.get`): runs `bin/claudetto-hygiene` (temp, branch, and trash sweep), capped at 10 minutes per run.
  - [ ] `line-file` (`session.measure`): writes the compaction window and theme for the status line.
  - [ ] `memory` (`tool.check`, `agent.spawn`, `session.usage`): holds a call under memory pressure, plus a session tree.
  - [ ] `price-step` (`turn.step`): bounds a Haiku request below its 5× price tier.
  - [ ] `prompt` (`prompt.compose`): the root prompt; Haiku gets no delegation block.
  - [ ] `protected` (`tool.check`): denies writes the sandbox would refuse in a project folder (list measured on 2.1.323).
  - [ ] `repeat` (`process.run`, `session.end`): points back to an earlier long output in place of sending it again.
  - [ ] `reply` (`command.run`, `ui.selection`, `prompt.fill`): `/reply` quotes part of a reply.
  - [ ] `secrets` (`session.cwd`, `session.root`): a secret scan.
  - [ ] `setup` (`command.run`, `skill.prompt`, `tool.register`): `/claudetto:setup` writes settings, starters, ignore blocks, rules, and auto-mode rules.
  - [ ] `stop-notice` (`ui.notify`): notifies when an API error ends a turn.
  - [ ] `surface` (`tool.describe`, `tool.list`, `prompt.attachment`): trims tool descriptions and defers tools; `Read`, `Write`, and `ListAgents` stay loaded.
  - [ ] `temp-cleanup` (`session.end`): removes the session's temp files.
  - [ ] `web` (`process.run`): a `web_fetch` tool.
- [ ] Agents: `Artemis` (search), `Athena` (review), `Heracles` (edit), `Metis` (plan).
- [ ] The `Concise` output style; the `/claudetto:setup` command; the `docs` skill.
- [ ] 11 rule files copied to `~/.claude/rules/claudetto/`: `commentOnlyWhy`, `findFactsByExperiment`, `guardOnlyTrustBoundaries`, `lookForClone`, `scopeEditsToGoal`, `useBlockLanguage`, `useEdgeCaseReport`, `useHeadlineseNames`, `useLeastCode`, `useOneObviousWay`, `useTestPatterns`.
- [ ] The status line (`statusline/`, `bin/claudetto-statusline`): rows, segments, limits, and colour (off under `NO_COLOR`).
- [ ] `claudetto-openspec`: an OpenSpec MCP tool and skills (apply, archive, explore, propose, sync, update, verify).
- [ ] `claudetto-you-should-know`: a side check every few steps that shows a note above the prompt. Tool calls are logged, but none is held.
- [ ] `tools/`: bench, fake-api, handover-probe, claim-check-*, mutate-command, prompt-bytes, release-version, and assorted repo tests.
- [ ] `tests/claudetto`, `tests/claudetto-openspec`.
- [ ] 79 hyphenated source files (`find plugins tools -name '*-*.ts'`), which the rebuild turns into real directories.

## Violations

Each one is something the agent did. The cure is listed beside it.

- [ ] It took a handoff or goal file as orders. Cure: only the user's own messages set work; `/goal` belongs to the user.
- [ ] It looked into the `openspec/` deletion and searched transcripts and `~/.Trash` without being asked, then did it again in another form. Cure: look only at what the user names.
- [ ] It started the rebuild after being told to stop. Cure: a stop ends the turn.
- [ ] It narrated between tool calls and added meta-commentary about its own replies. Cure: text between calls holds a finding, a result, or an error.
- [ ] It opened by agreeing or apologising, and confessed and promised, including in the reply meant to list exactly that fault. Cure: open with the checked fact.
- [ ] It ended a reply by asking the user to direct the work. Cure: end on the result or the blocker.
- [ ] It chased gamed benchmark scores (`ed265246`, `681376b6`, mutation scoring, targets set to `pass: true`). Cure: product first; run only the narrowest check that proves a part works.
- [ ] It reran tests and checks in place of building the product.
- [ ] It kept making tool calls after a mid-turn correction. Cure: answer the correction first.
- [ ] It skipped the "wait" step of a handoff.
- [ ] It read past the read cap, and the read was cut off. Cure: read in slices.
- [ ] It ran `cd` in place of using absolute paths.
- [ ] A handoff stated git state it never checked: HEAD, the unpushed count, and the file count.
- [ ] It quoted the user verbatim in files.
- [ ] It deleted things nobody asked it to remove (`fold.ts`, the task panel). It also restored sections on an unchecked claim.
- [ ] It handed the user steps it could do itself, and asked questions a free experiment would settle.
- [ ] It filled a missing fact with a guess: the wrong session credited, a lint result read from `tail` alone, inferences left unlabelled.
- [ ] It named a check and did not run it, and once called a check free that only echoed a script.
- [ ] It read with `sed`, `head`, or `cat` in place of `Read`, and read one file of a 42-file folder.
- [ ] It ended on work it said it would do next, though it could do that work now.
- [ ] It gave one worker two tasks. Five parallel workers each ran type-aware `oxlint`, about 16 GB per run, and filled RAM.
- [ ] It left finished agents idle and started new ones.
- [ ] It labelled the user's words a "ruling" and narrowed them.
- [ ] It claimed "done" with no check after the last edit.
- [ ] It called a tool named `bash`; tool names are case-sensitive, so the call failed with "No such tool available". Cure: copy each tool name from its schema.
- [ ] It marked `experimental.cacheTtl` absent from a `surface` query of kind `setting`, which lists settings only, and wrote that mark into two files; the key exists as agent frontmatter (chunk-wqb2b4v0.js:994409). Cure: mark a name absent only after a literal `code` search of the whole build finds nothing.
- [ ] It wrote a forward reference to a "User Answers" section that did not exist yet. Cure: write the target before the reference.
- [ ] It ran `grep -o` with a long `.{0,200}` window, which ugrep refuses as too complex, and macOS `cat -A`, which BSD `cat` lacks. Cure: record the userland once (grep is ugrep, BSD tools) and use `perl` for context windows.

- [ ] Rebuild top down: `plugin.json` and the hook entry, then hooks, scripts, statusline, rules, agents, output style, commands, and skills; then `claudetto-you-should-know`, the other plugins, `tools/`, and `tests/`.
- [ ] Use real directories and no hyphenated file families. Delete each old path when its replacement lands.
- [ ] Give every conduct cure above a mechanism (a hook, a deny, a Stop check, a subagent context), not prose alone.
- [ ] Use the dossier as the spec, with the curated items below as its index.

## Builtins (Measured on 2.1.325)

Fourteen builtins register in 2.1.325: `cc-plugin-sec-default`, `cc-plugin-agents-md`, `cc-plugin-telemetry`, `cc-plugin-privacy`, `cc-plugin-plugin-authoring`, `cc-plugin-mods-guide`, `cc-plugin-tips`, `cc-plugin-mermaid`, `cc-plugin-responsive-mode`, `cc-plugin-diff`, `cc-plugin-you-should-know`, `cc-plugin-easel`, `claude-platform`, and `cc-plugin-claude-test`, each with the id `<name>@builtin`.

Enabled-state algorithm (`yY()`, `chunk-yjs2ay06.js@72674`):

```
for each registered builtin b where b.isAvailable():
  id = b.name + "@builtin"
  v = b.enabledFromPolicyOnly ? managedSettings.enabledPlugins[id]
    : b.enabledFromTrustedSettingsOnly ? trustedSettings.enabledPlugins[id]   # trusted = userSettings, flagSettings, policy; project only after trust
    : mergedSettings.enabledPlugins[id]
  enabled = v !== undefined ? v === true : (b.defaultEnabled ?? true)
```

- `sec-default` is policy-only and available only on a machine with managed settings or a Team or Enterprise org, so a user setting cannot change it.
- `privacy` is trusted-only and `defaultEnabled: true`. `responsive-mode` and `you-should-know` are `defaultEnabled: false`.
- A plugin cannot refuse a builtin at runtime. `plugin.register` hooks may answer `{ refuse }`, but a native plugin is judged only by native plugins, and a non-user plugin is not judged by user-tier plugins (`ruo()`, `chunk-wqb2b4v0.js@4661039`).

Probes (cc-inspect `cli`, `-p go`, stream-json init event):

| Setup | Init `plugins` | Builtin traces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Vanilla | `agents-md`, `privacy`, `plugin-authoring` | skill text in request 1, `plugins/store/cc-plugin-agents-md_builtin-*.json` |
| `--settings` with all 14 ids `false` | `[]` | none |
| Project `.claude/settings.json` with `agents-md`, `plugin-authoring` `false` | not captured | none |
| Plugin-shipped `settings.json` with the same keys | not captured | still present |

- [ ] Enforce the disable in setup: write `enabledPlugins["<id>@builtin"] = false` for all 14 ids (user decision: all 14, `privacy` included) into user settings, a trusted scope that also covers `privacy`.
- [ ] Enforce it at runtime (user decision: rewrite settings): at session start, read user settings, and if any of the 14 ids is not `false`, write `false` for it into user settings. No runtime refusal path exists. Measured (cc-inspect `cli`, a `tool.call` hook writes `<config-dir>/settings.json` before a `Skill plugin-authoring` call): with a 3 s wait after the write, `Skill` returns "Unknown skill: plugin-authoring"; with no wait, in two lead runs, the skill still launched. The watcher reads the file about 1.2 to 1.5 s after the write (debug log: write 18:55:40.810, "Settings changed from userSettings" 18:55:41.965; interactive 1.5 s). From then on, builtin skills and commands are gone; `yY()` re-reads settings on each call (`chunk-yjs2ay06.js@72522`). A builtin hooks module keeps running: after the change, `cc-plugin-agents-md@builtin` still settled `tool.call`, `prompt.submit`, and `turn.*` (lead, headless), because the hooks hot-reload on a `userSettings` change runs only when its tier snapshot changed (`chunk-wqb2b4v0.js@4789330`). An interactive `/reload-plugins` unloads it ("hooks modules unloaded: cc-plugin-agents-md@builtin (plugin disabled or uninstalled)"), and a relaunch never loads it (worker, interactive, raw logs checked by lead). Headless `/reload-plugins` refuses ("isn't available over a remote connection in this session"). The skill listing already sent in request 1 stays in history until the next clear. Builtin hooks modules seen loaded on 2.1.325 (debug log, vanilla plus the interactive run): `agents-md` (session.start, prompt.context, command.run, session.compact, prompt.submit, turn.*), `plugin-authoring` (skill.prompt, tool.check, tool.call), `privacy` (tool.check), `diff` (session.start, ui.*, command.run, tool.*). So: setup writes the disable once, and every later launch loads no builtin. The session-start check repairs a drift by writing `false` and telling the user to run `/reload-plugins` (interactive) or restart (headless), because a hooks module of a re-enabled builtin is already running.
- [ ] Keep the id list in one constant, and diff it against the registration list on each Claude Code release.

## Perl Batch Edit (Measured on 2.1.325)

- [ ] One `Bash` call `perl -0pi -e 's/.../.../g' a b c` replaces many `Edit` calls. Under `acceptEdits` it is denied: "The following part requires approval: perl -0pi -e ...".
- [ ] With `permissions.allow: ["Bash(perl -0pi -e *)"]`, the call runs with no prompt (`permission_decision: accept, source: config`), and the files change.
- [ ] Setup writes that allow rule. (user decision: allow rule plus the diff hook.) The rule bypasses the `Read`-before-`Edit` guard and the `Edit` diff view, so the cure for blind edits is a `PostToolUse` hook on `Bash(perl -0pi*)` that prints `git diff --stat` of the touched files.
- [ ] A custom perl tool through `$.tool.register` is the alternative; it needs its own permission check and adds a tool schema to each request. Prefer the `Bash` rule.

## User Answers

Answers given through `AskUserQuestion` on 2026-11-16.

- [ ] Builtins: disable all 14, `privacy` included. Enforce by rewriting user settings at session start.
- [ ] Perl: setup writes the `Bash(perl -0pi -e *)` allow rule, plus a diff hook after each such call.
- [ ] `Athena` stays on `claude-sonnet-5-5`.
- [ ] `autoMode`: replace the built-in lists (no `"$defaults"`), to shrink the classifier prompt.
- [ ] Advisor: stays off (`CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL=1`), because it costs too much.
- [ ] Opener Stop check: dropped. A word list or regex judge is fragile.
- [ ] Constraint ledger: dropped.
- [ ] Compaction: replace auto-compaction with a self-managed session cycle. The agent writes a handoff, clears the session, and resumes from the handoff, without breaking the user's own `/clear`. The trigger is an idle gap or context size, not session age: each cache read refreshes the TTL, which runs from the start of each request (platform docs, prompt-caching, "How prompt caching works"), so a 3 h session with steady turns stays warm, and a gap of 1 h or more leaves the whole prefix cold. See Session Cycle.

## Conflicts Settled (Measured on 2.1.325)

Each line settles a conflict that two or more sections record. The lead read the code with cc-inspect; `settings.ts` means `plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/setup/settings.ts` at `088a227c`.

- [ ] **Subagent cache TTL.** Both claims hold. With nothing set, a subscriber gets 1 h only for the query sources in the allowlist `repl_main_thread*`, `sdk`, `auto_mode`, `memdir_relevance` (GrowthBook `tengu_prompt_cache_1h_config` can widen it), so subagents default to 5 min (`rNe`, chunk-wqb2b4v0.js:3067022). Precedence: `FORCE_PROMPT_CACHING_5M`, then `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`, then `subagentPromptCacheTtl`, then agent frontmatter `experimental.cacheTtl` (its "1h" is ignored in overage), then `ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H` (`cIn`, chunk-wqb2b4v0.js:2438231). Rebuild: one way, the setting.
- [ ] **Version floor.** `requiredMinimumVersion` makes an older Claude Code exit at startup (chunk-gywtf5sd.js:281772); `minimumVersion` only blocks a downgrade on the stable channel (:281644). `settings.ts:146` writes `minimumVersion`, so the old floor never held. Rebuild: `requiredMinimumVersion` 2.1.325.
- [ ] **Silent-turn reminder.** `CLAUDE_CODE_SILENT_TURN_REMINDER` is a tri-state switch; `CLAUDE_CODE_SILENT_TURN_REMINDER_TEXT` only replaces the text, whose default comes from GrowthBook `tengu_hushed_lark_text` (chunk-wqb2b4v0.js:3789361). They are not alternatives. Rebuild: `"0"` alone, as `settings.ts:188` writes, with `_TEXT` retired.
- [ ] **autoMode `$defaults`.** A list without `"$defaults"` replaces the built-in rules; `"$defaults"` splices them in at its first position (`DZn`, chunk-sk365049.js:435). Both reports describe the same code. The choice is recorded under User Answers.
- [ ] **Rule count.** `plugins/claudetto/rules/` holds 11 files at `088a227c` (`ls`); 7 and 8 are older counts. The rebuild count follows the trim.
- [ ] **Haiku `autoCompactWindow`.** `settings.ts:114` writes `100_000` for Haiku; 200000 comes from an outside post. The price step cited for 100000 is unverified. Rebuild: 100000 until a pricing page read settles it.
- [ ] **Advisor.** `settings.ts:178` writes `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL=1`, while `/advisor` exists in 2.1.325 (chunk-wqb2b4v0.js:2399316). The choice is recorded under User Answers.
- [ ] **Athena model, compaction handoff, constraint ledger.** `Athena.md:4` pins `claude-sonnet-5-5`; the handoff ships in `hooks/features/compaction/handoff.ts`; the ledger was removed after 132 denials in 3 days, 8 of them false. The choices are recorded under User Answers.

## Session Cycle (Measured on 2.1.325)

Replaces auto-compaction (User Answers). The agent writes a handoff, clears the session, and resumes from the handoff.

Cache fact (platform docs, prompt-caching, "How prompt caching works"): "The cache is refreshed for no additional cost each time the cached content is used", and the lifetime runs from the start of each request. So session age never cools the cache; an idle gap of the TTL or longer does.

Probes (cc-inspect `cli`, a `--plugin-dir` module, one stream-json prompt; the worker's runs plus one lead re-run):

| Call | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `$.command.run({command:'clear'})` unawaited at main-loop `turn.complete` | clears: `first one` only in request 1; session id changes; `session.end` `{reason:"clear", sessionId:<old>}` |
| then `$.prompt.submit({text, asUser:true})` | `text` is the first user content of request 2, bare; without `asUser` it arrives framed as "The probe plugin sent a message" |
| clear while another prompt is queued | rejects: "HooksError: the command was removed from the queue before it ran" |
| `session.start` after the clear | does not fire |
| `$.session.compact()` headless | rejects: "not available in a headless (-p / SDK) session yet" |
| `$.session.compact()` unawaited at `turn.complete`, interactive TUI, fake API stub (worker) | runs; the hook gets `{trigger:"plugin", messages:[...]}`; its answer `{messages:[handoff]}` becomes request 2 (`HANDOFF MSG` plus the next prompt, no earlier text); same transcript file, so the session id is kept; no summary request is sent |
| `session.compact` hook answering `{messages:[handoff]}` on a typed `/compact` | request 2 holds the handoff, not the history; session id kept |
| `command.run` origin | plugin: `{kind:"plugin", name}`; typed in stream-json: `{kind:"sdk"}`; typed in the TUI: `{kind:"composer"}` (worker, interactive); a typed `/clear` starts a new transcript |

- [ ] Trigger, checked at main-loop `turn.complete` (`e.agentId === undefined`): context size over a threshold. Check at `prompt.submit` too: when the time since the last request start is the TTL or more, the prefix is cold, so the next request pays it uncached either way and a cycle costs least then. Never trigger on session age.
- [ ] Headless mechanism (`-p`, SDK): write the handoff (a fork's reply plus `git rev-parse --short HEAD` and `git status --short`, as `handoff.ts` builds it), then `$.command.run({command:'clear'})` and, once it resolves, `$.prompt.submit({text: handoff, asUser: true})`. Leave both unawaited in the hook, because the turn waits on its hooks and a command run inside one rejects.
- [ ] On a rejected clear (a queued prompt), keep the handoff and retry at the next `turn.complete`.
- [ ] State reset goes on `session.end` with `reason === "clear"`, because `session.start` does not fire after a clear.
- [ ] Superseded by Handover (a `command.run` hook on `clear` writes the handover, then runs the clear unchanged). Old item: keep the user's `/clear` vanilla: register no `command.run` hook that changes it; a hook that must tell them apart passes through when `e.origin.kind !== "plugin"`.
- [ ] Interactive path: `$.session.compact()` plus a `session.compact` hook that answers `{messages:[handoff]}` for trigger `plugin` keeps the session id and sends no summary request, so it is the primary mechanism in the TUI. Headless (`-p`, SDK) it rejects, so there the cycle uses `clear` plus `prompt.submit`.
- [ ] The `/clear` command record stays in front of the handoff in request 2; the handoff text must not repeat it.
- [ ] Medical error at `088a227c`: `handoff.ts:67-80` compacts when 59 minutes have passed since session start or the last compaction, which measures age, so it cycles a warm session and keeps a cold one. Cure: the trigger above.
- [ ] `$.fs.write` has no append option: a call with `{append:true}` overwrote the file (lead probe). Keep a log as an array and write it whole.

## Handover (Measured on 2.1.325)

Handover is claudetto's cheap replacement for compaction: a short fork-written note carries the work across a clear or a compaction. It extends Session Cycle and supersedes its vanilla-`/clear` item.

Knobs mirror Claude Code's compaction surface (cc-inspect `surface` `setting`/`env`, 2.1.325), with "compact" read as "handover" and `CLAUDE_CODE_*` as `CLAUDETTO_*`. Each default mirrors Claude Code's; `idleCompaction` documents `true`.

| Claude Code | claudetto | Use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `autoCompactEnabled` | `autoHandoverEnabled` | auto handover on or off |
| `autoCompactWindow` | `autoHandoverWindow` | window the threshold is measured against |
| `idleCompaction` | `idleHandover` | handover while idle |
| `precomputeCompactionEnabled` | `precomputeHandoverEnabled` | write the handover in the `precompute` pass |
| `CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE` | `CLAUDETTO_AUTOHANDOVER_PCT_OVERRIDE` | threshold percent |
| `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` | `CLAUDETTO_AUTO_HANDOVER_WINDOW` | window, accepts `k`/`M` |
| `CLAUDE_CODE_IDLE_COMPACT_MIN_TOKENS` | `CLAUDETTO_IDLE_HANDOVER_MIN_TOKENS` | idle floor |
| `CLAUDE_CODE_SLEEP_COMPACT` | `CLAUDETTO_SLEEP_HANDOVER` | timer at 0.9 of the cache TTL |
| `CLAUDE_CODE_SLEEP_COMPACT_AFTER_MS` | `CLAUDETTO_SLEEP_HANDOVER_AFTER_MS` | timer, clamped 60000-210000 |
| `CLAUDE_CODE_COLD_COMPACT` | `CLAUDETTO_COLD_HANDOVER` | handover when the prefix is cold (Session Cycle `prompt.submit` check) |
| `DISABLE_AUTO_COMPACT` | `CLAUDETTO_DISABLE_AUTO_HANDOVER` | auto path off, manual `/clear` handover stays |
| `DISABLE_COMPACT` | `CLAUDETTO_DISABLE_HANDOVER` | all handover off |
| `CLAUDE_AFTER_LAST_COMPACT`, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_PRECOMPACT_SKIP`, `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_MODE_COMPACT_THREAD_RULE` | none | core internals with no handover meaning |

Probes (cc-inspect `cli`, fake API, stream-json prompts `first one`, `/clear`, `after clear`, plugin under `--plugin-dir`):

| Probe | Result |
| --- | --- |
| A: `command.run` hook on `{command:'clear'}` awaits `$.model.fork`, stores the text, returns `next(e)`; `prompt.context` appends block `probe:handover` | 3 requests; the fork is request 2 and holds `first one`; request 3 opens with `# probe:handover` plus the handover and holds no `first one`; cache_control on `messages[1].content[0]` in request 1, `messages[2].content[0]` in the fork |
| B: same hook reads `$.session.messages()` instead | `n=2`, rows `{role,text,toolUses}`, origin `{kind:"sdk"}`; 2 requests, request 2 carries the extract |
| C: knobs | `$.settings.read()` returns custom keys (`autoHandoverEnabled`, `idleHandover`); `$.env.get` reads `CLAUDETTO_*` from the process env and from settings `env` |
| D: `session.compact` answering `{messages}` on trigger `auto`, fake API | not reachable: the stub always reports 10 input tokens, `replies` does not override `POST /v1/messages`, and `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` below 100k reads as the 100k minimum (`G0=1e5`, chunk-5121ahsh.js:950) |
| E (live, paid, Haiku 5.5, `claude -p` stream-json): 60000-word `prompt.context` filler, then `/clear`, then a prompt | the `clear` hook's fork took 2401 ms and returned 365 characters; fork usage 67542 cache-read, 5248 cache-write, 120 output tokens, $0.0018, against $0.0135 for the first turn; the next turn quoted `HANDOVER-CLEAR forkMs=2401`, so the `$.store` handover survived the clear and `prompt.context` injected it |
| F (live, paid): 110000-word filler, `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW=100k`, two prompts | `compact_boundary` `{trigger:"auto", pre_tokens:122980, post_tokens:275, duration_ms:4793}`; the installed `claudetto@claudetto-dev` compaction hook answered first with its fork handoff, so the probe's own `session.compact` hook never ran; `prompt.context` fired again after the compaction and re-sent the filler (second turn $0.24) |

Code read (cc-inspect `read`, chunk-wqb2b4v0.js): the reactive (prompt-too-long) path calls the `session.compact` hooks with `trigger:"auto"` and `agentId` (:2497458); a hook's `{messages}` (`kind:"replaced"`) becomes the replacement (:2499078); a `{skip}` returns `{result:null,hookBlocked:true}` with notice "not compacted" (:2498600). The `precompute` pass takes a hook's `{messages}` as a ready replacement and logs "ready with a hook's N messages"; a `{skip}` records a veto (:2871600).

- [ ] Manual `/clear`: a `command.run` hook on `{command:'clear'}` writes the handover, keeps it, then returns `next(e)`, so the clear runs unchanged after the write. A `prompt.context` block injects it into the first request after the clear, with no extra request (probe A).
- [ ] Writer: `$.model.fork({prompt, effort:'low'})`, a tool-less completion over the cached prefix with a small output, then `git rev-parse --short HEAD` and `git status --short` appended.
- [ ] Fallback writer: a `$.session.messages()` extract (zero model cost, probe B) on `reason:'nothing-to-fork'`, a thrown fork, or empty text.
- [ ] Origin skip: pass through via `next(e)` when `e.origin.kind === "plugin"` and the name is claudetto, because the headless cycle already wrote its handover before its own clear.
- [ ] Pending handover lives in `$.store`, not module memory, because `/reload-plugins` re-runs `register` and drops module state between the clear and the next prompt. Clear it once `prompt.context` has injected it.
- [ ] Show a toast (`$.ui.notify`) while the fork runs, because the clear waits on it: 2.4 s on Haiku 5.5 over a 67k-token cached prefix (probe E). Hook time stops during `$` calls, so the 10 s hook budget does not cut the fork.
- [ ] `$.env.get` returns a promise: an unawaited read gave `NaN` and an empty filler (probe E, first runs). Await every `$.env.get`.
- [ ] `prompt.context` fires again after a compaction (probe F), so each block is paid again after it. Keep blocks small, and inject the pending handover there after a compaction too.
- [ ] Only one plugin's `session.compact` answer wins: the first hook that answers without `next(e)` ends the chain (probe F). Uninstall `claudetto@claudetto-dev` before dogfooding the rebuild, and keep handover in one module.
- [ ] Medical error at `088a227c`: the handoff's `Git now:` line prints empty backticks in a repo with no commits, because `git rev-parse --short HEAD` fails there (probe F transcript). Cure: print `no commits` when it fails.
- [ ] Core auto compaction while handover is on: a `session.compact` hook with trigger `auto` or `precompute` and no `agentId` answers `{messages:[handover]}`, never `{skip}`, because a skip on a prompt-too-long leaves the context full and the turn fails (code read above). The `precompute` pass writes the fork ahead of the threshold, so its latency is hidden. Subagent compactions (`agentId` set), trigger `manual`, and handover off go to `next(e)`.
- [ ] Knob reads: settings keys through `$.settings.read()`, env through `$.env.get` (probe C). `CLAUDETTO_DISABLE_HANDOVER` turns off every path above; `CLAUDETTO_DISABLE_AUTO_HANDOVER` keeps the manual `/clear` handover.

<!-- Sections below are merged from the curation workers. -->

## Medical Errors

Each item below is an agent fault written as a medical error: a disease with a diagnosis, a mechanism, and a cure. The rebuild treats each one as a condition to cure across subsystems (prompts, hooks, agents, context, cost, verification), not as a one-time patch. Source tags such as `[3]` give the input line of `faults-all.txt`; the letters after them are the original theme and finding tags.

### Sycophancy and Pushback

- [ ] **Sycophantic Fold.** Diagnosis: the agent opens with agreement ("You're right", "absolutely right", "excellent observation", "Great point", an apology), drops a correct position or edits a correct test to the user's number under pushback, yields with no new evidence, then repeats the same wrong claim later; objections soften after pushback or by message 30-40 and turn into a template; it agrees when pushed on settings advice and said "You're right" twice in a row. Mechanism: preference-trained agreement is the cheapest reply; pressure grows collapse though the correct answer stays in the reasoning trace (cited [2310.13548], [2609.09090]); 175 "you're right" in ~1,900 sessions (counted); a hook built earlier may not have been active; word lists failed (10 of 12 evasions passed). Cure: Stop prompt hook on the first sentence (SA-3.3, agree-opener check at wording level, anchored deterministic test) plus a pushback rule: weigh a disputed claim as a third party's, check it in the same turn, open with the fact, change position only on named new evidence; claim check raised syc-fixed-total 9/16 -> 16/16 (p=0.0068, measured); prompt-only line 14/24 -> 17/24 (noise); UserPromptSubmit hook re-adds a checkable rule (list the two strongest objections before agreeing; after pushback say what new evidence changed the answer); no Stop-time model judge; test that the hook is registered (single hook table). Source: [3] A, B, C, D, E, G, H, I; [65] A L13, D L108; [147] L51, L78 (W5-18), L233, L234; [160] A, B, H (reported; mechanism inferred). have (from [3]): partial prompt text `plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/prompt/texts.ts:15`; Stop hook missing.
- [ ] **Unfounded Pushback and Pedantry.** Diagnosis: the agent invents problems, argues with things the user did not say, lectures or moralises ("I'd push back on ..."), refuses a blanket "rewrite in X" with an unmeasured claim (then a 10x speedup and 80% less RAM after a correct benchmark), over-suspects and redacts, says "I can't verify that", and also folds quickly under challenge; a "Claude is less yes-man" signal exists, so both sycophancy and pedantry live. Mechanism: reported, no mechanism proven. Cure: profile or measure before judging, list assumptions, one short stance block, restart a stuck moralising session. Source: [29] E, F, H, I (reported).
- [ ] **Confession Instead of Fact.** Diagnosis: the reply apologizes, names its own mistake with no result, uses "my mistake" / "I should not have", states feelings, tells realizations, repeats the person's words, lists steps as a report; R52 names this "Claudeism": cognitive obfuscation, rhetorical evasion, user-mediated functionality; Opus orchestrating Opus loops on "I caught myself doing something wrong, fixed and documented"; the person's stance is that a coding agent speaks facts. Mechanism: reply-register habit and reply-style defaults, not task state; narrated work without new evidence. Cure: typed Stop questions per fault scored on stored replies (see Keep: shipped at >=80% precision/recall) through Decider (five shipped questions, any yes blocks once, `stop_hook_active` passes); `<register>` block in the output style; bug-report reply recipe; Stop check on self-referential confession text; lead on a different model. Source: [8] B, C, D, F; [87] B:32; D:106,107; [55] E (reported).
- [ ] **Credit Misassignment.** Diagnosis: the agent credits the user with labels from its own script or unstated choices; keeps a guessed term in assumptions after the user defined it, or claims "I've redefined"; keeps an environment explanation after the user reports a verified fix; substitutes terms semantically. Mechanism: no state check of who said what. Cure: Stop codes `credited`/`unseen` (held-out recall 12/12, 0/30 false flags after rewording; first 10/19); replace everywhere and credit the user's definition; pinned glossary. Source: [9] B, C, I.

### Reply Register

- [ ] **Narrated Work.** Diagnosis: replies carry "Now I will read the file" and "I'll start by reading the config file." lines before or between tool calls, tool-by-tool recaps, padding, doctor-chart narration, "Important caveat worth knowing" with no caveat, stock words ("load-bearing", "blast radius", "The honest answer", "Perfect", emoji sign-off, "oops"), professor register, negative parallelism, em-dash substitution; reply text lost into hidden turns; the final reply shown twice after a Stop-hook block (observed once, 60fc9776, `isMeta` block text, cause unfixed). Mechanism: the server silent-turn reminder asks the model to say in a few words what it is doing and to keep the user updated each turn; large system prompts overwhelm output styles; announcing text substitutes for action; the next tool call already shows itself; style leak (reported). Cure: `CLAUDE_CODE_SILENT_TURN_REMINDER_TEXT` override asking one sentence on a finding or result only (effect not significant; setup env at `setup/settings.ts:202`), output style, end-of-turn hook demanding a plain re-summary, describe the tic in the system prompt; outcome-first recipe (I12); Stop/PostToolUse/`turn.step` check on the first assistant block before a tool call (eval `quiet-when-told`, start-with-tool-call case); report template with no recap; Concise rule requiring outcome-first. Source: [7] A, B, C, D, E, G, I; [128] B surface-trim; [148] L51, L228; [161] B. 2.1.325: env present (from [7] and [128]). have: [128] setup/settings.ts:202; [161] have: partial.
- [ ] **Narrated Duplicate Report.** Diagnosis: after a Stop block the agent resends the whole reply, so one report shows twice in two wordings that can disagree. Mechanism: the block text asks for a reply again without saying the screen keeps the first. Cure: `KEEP_ANSWER` suffix on each re-ask block; blocks that name lines name the edit instead; no hook writes a SendMessage result into a reply. Source: [68] B L47, D L118.
- [ ] **Swallowed Reply.** Diagnosis: the answer sits in thinking and the turn ends with no visible text before a tool call. Mechanism: reported Opus 5.5 behaviour (issues 96288, 97504, 94354). Cure: a Stop hook that parses the transcript, flags a last assistant message without visible text and blocks the stop with a restate request. Source: [129] A, E (reported). have: missing.
- [ ] **Style Leak and Verbosity.** Diagnosis: a 124-word reply to "which button should I click next", rambling, "Claudish" prose (personification, "it is not this, it is that", coined jargon), AI-code readability not enforced, README written for agents, a "stop saying load-bearing" rule added unprompted, jargon with no gloss, explanatory evasion. Mechanism: style defaults of the model. Cure: positive definition of concise, keep-list, humanizer skill, README for humans and AGENTS.md for agents, `jargon` code (removed in change 2). Source: [34] A, B, C, E, G, I.
- [ ] **Verdict-Worded Tool Result.** Diagnosis: the model tells the user something false after a result worded "NOT SENT, domain cannot receive email" (n=3, reported). Mechanism: verdict text read as fact; sycophancy toward the tool text. Cure: tool result texts as plain facts plus next action. Source: [134] A (n=3). have: repeat/texts.ts, memory/texts.ts, protected/texts.ts.
- [ ] **Narration in Shipped Source.** Diagnosis: session history ("added during task 3", "as discussed", "the user decided") written into source; 218 added comments in 1,610 edits is tracked under Instruction Decay. Mechanism: the agent logs its own session where it works. Cure: PreToolUse `Write|Edit` provenance guard on added lines (ask by default, `AGENT_TERMS_PROVENANCE_MODE=deny`), plus a FileChanged hook or CI lint for Bash writes. Source: [88] A:12.
- [ ] **Comment Inflation.** Diagnosis: later edits copy a long comment onto every new line. Mechanism: the system-prompt line to match comment density, naming and idiom copies the first comment pattern. Cure: a global CLAUDE.md line restating the default and the wanted behavior, plus a fence line separating it from the system prompt; a comment rule in the plugin prompt. Source: [71] G L218 (reported). partial.

### False Completion

- [ ] **False Completion.** Diagnosis: "done", "fixed", "passes", "Confirmed", "should work", "tests pass", "all issues addressed" with nothing run, including "Task 23.2 is done" after a rejected edit; fake-API or mock-only proof; deferred steps relabelled "intentionally deferred"; partial work reported proudly; a failed check hidden by stderr to /dev/null or a pipe; "Prettier in a Biome repo", a shim reported done, 161 vs 159 tasks, "validates the same" with 5 new warnings, 7 of 9 handoff sections empty. Mechanism: self-certification without external evidence; the report is written from intent, not evidence; prose closes a turn without a check; a delegate report is treated as evidence; model-stated motives are rationalization. Cure: Stop gate on a real check (`unchecked` fault) that runs the project check and blocks until command and exit code are quoted; "done" must list checked and skipped; `pipefail` gate; fresh reviewer; TaskCompleted hook with exit 2; SubagentStop check of the hand-back form; `git status` end-of-turn check; blockers verified, not worded; orchestrator re-verification plus evidence-substring rule; no prompt proven to remove it (research doc). Source: [4] A, B, C, E, G, H, I; [80] A:2,6,13,14,193; [103] A, D321; [145] L19, L78 (W5-10), L128, L229.
- [ ] **Self-Grading Without External Evidence.** Diagnosis: the agent grades itself and reports it as measured; skipped files in 67.9% of runs, 80.4% misleading (cited 2609.20812); 100% self-reported vs 25% real (reported); 7.8% of "solved" patches fail developer tests (cited); its own "tests pass" or a subagent report counts as evidence; half-finished work reported done; a workflow run reports "completed" while a thread failed or was not created; 49 tasks auto-closed, 2 on evidence (reported); a scheduled-run summary graded by the agent missed a revoked token. Mechanism: self-correction without external feedback fails (cited 2310.01798); the grader is the actor; status derived from narration; prompt-type Stop judge has 0 tools and sees transcript text only; completion gates fail open (`onFailure` ignored on Stop/SubagentStop/TaskCompleted/TeammateIdle; Stop blocks capped at 8 in a row); agent-type hooks default to Haiku. Cure: fresh-context reviewer subagent (Athena) or a Stop hook measuring from exit codes and full output; execution status mechanical, coverage "n of N" its own field, agent never sets its own done bit; verifier hooks set `model` explicitly and gate on exit codes and files, not prose; root runs the delegated check on report arrival; Stop check for a statement about code with no workspace read in the turn; Stop check computing coverage from transcripts, missing required evidence fails the run; task close requires a recorded check output (hook on TaskUpdate/Stop); independent hook listing failed or never-fired runs over 7 days plus an out-of-band check. Source: [47] A, G; [64] A L12, B L24, E L148, G L213, D L119; [48] G (cited docs); [136] I (reported); [99] I:169.
- [ ] **Skipped-Part Completion.** Diagnosis: Claude declares done while skipping or deflecting part of the task ("pre-existing", "out of scope"); a case with baseline 1.00 reported as pass, a case changed after results, shipping after thin samples; a single-environment assumption lives only in the writer's session. Mechanism: skipped parts are often the untested ones; the assumption is not recorded. Cure: Stop hook checking each documented instruction and unrun tests, anti-deflection pattern detector, independent QA subagent that sees only the issue text; grader that outputs "case does not separate the arms" when baseline equals arm; Stop check requiring a stated-assumptions block and test output from a CI-matching environment. Source: [117] H233, I289, I292, H250; [166] A; [167] G. have (from [167]): missing.
- [ ] **Premature Stop.** Diagnosis: the agent stops with "I'll finish the project files now" and does nothing, tells the user to ship failing tests, ends turns citing time, announces the next step, offers to continue, lists non-blocking decisions; compaction injected continue text so it ran past a plan-approval wait; it wakes up and drops the task after a wait; an API error mid-turn lost an 8-hour run; a classifier denial or auto-mode block (git commit, cd) makes it stop instead of finding another route; a session ran until the whole plan was done after the batch; Haiku/Sonnet at low effort report done unchecked. Mechanism: effort and model tendencies (cited Anthropic guides). Cure: Stop open-items check; Stop hook that blocks a stop whose last message announces next work, offers to continue, or claims done with no verifying tool call; heartbeat wakeup ~30 min that disarms on completion; retry/backoff (cited docs advise exponential backoff); fresh session (inferred); effort floor medium in agent files (agents carry `effort:`). Source: [25] A, C, E, G, H; [130] A Table 6. have (from [25]): partial `plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/stop-notice/`.
- [ ] **Tool Call as Text.** Diagnosis: with thinking disabled a tool call is written as text and never runs, while the reply says "done". Mechanism: the model emits call syntax in prose. Cure: Stop hook scanning the last reply for call syntax with no matching `tool_use` block. Source: [90] F:145.
- [ ] **Review Blindness.** Diagnosis: the review says fine, tests are green, the product is wrong; the writer's session approves its own code; a clean-looking patch holds a wrong cross-component assumption; green tests that do not run moved lines; fake or trivially passing LLM tests; LLM tests pinning wording (I15); first-pass Opus 5.5 gave incomplete conclusions until asked to check for regressions; a reviewing subagent echoes the main session's leaning and analysts follow the agent's hidden lean 86% (cited secondhand); inflated severity from one reviewer. Mechanism: shared context and shared-family blind spots; opinion leaks via the brief. Cure: spec defines proof before building, vertical slices, tests staged before implementation, context-free checker, behaviour/trace graders with fixtures, one write one check; SubagentStop/Task gate dispatching a fresh-context (other-family) reviewer given diff and requirements only, with a concrete failing case per finding; subagent context with facts only and a forced `supports plan` / `contradicts plan` / `insufficient evidence` answer. Source: [22] A, E, G; [54] H; [118] E4, E14, G175, F119, I290.
- [ ] **Review Loop Inflation.** Diagnosis: an adversarial reviewer always finds something (33 rounds of fixes), a same-prompt review loop overthinks (Sonnet 5.5 max effort: 300k thinking tokens, no output), 467 "leaked keys" where 1 was real; the reviewer grades the implementer's summary. Mechanism: asked to find bugs, it always will; no pass/fail criterion and no round cap; false completion by volume. Cure: review subagent context holds only acceptance criteria and the diff; reviewer contract requiring a repro (file, line, failing input) per finding and dropping findings without a failing test; cap rounds (1 pass, or 3 with leftovers listed); exit rule max N findings; effort high or medium. Source: [69] G L210; [165] G, E.
- [ ] **Invalid Measurement.** Diagnosis: a probe passed by exit code, a command that sends no Apple Event, a scorer reading the wrong results, a task suffix applied to every arm; the dev arm of a benchmark blocked by "Classifier unavailable" on every Bash call (fails closed, no test run); a full suite with `.venv/bin` on PATH rewrote a deliberately unsorted fixture that stayed in the diff; a run passing both API key and OAuth token billed the API (148 sessions $6.99; 186 sessions $6.74); earlier draft errors in the agent's own audit ("34.9% duplicated lines" was an overlapping-clone count; "five copies" was four; 6 of 11 "unused" exports had importers). measured. Cure: arm assertion, keep traces, discrimination check, a finish step that reads `git status` and restores files a check changed outside the change, check what a command did. Source: [23] A, B, C, D.

### Misread Results

- [ ] **Tool Error Read as Negative.** Diagnosis: a failed `pgrep`/`ps`/`ls`/`grep` is read as "no eval running" or "no file", causing a duplicate paid run; a "Not logged in" probe reported as failed instead of rerun with the token; a defect found and not reported; unknown state called absent. Mechanism: a tool error treated as an empty result. Cure: per-job lock file with PID and owner; `tool.check` on kill/start paths requiring the lock-record check; PostToolUseFailure hook that logs, marks state "unknown since <time>" and adds "state unknown, check another way"; `unfixed` fault offered when the last Bash result is an error (held-out recall 12/12, 0/12 false); `unfiled` (removed in change 2). Source: [39] A, C 18.38; [86] A:23,192; H:164; [158] A.
- [ ] **Trusting a Rejection Message.** Diagnosis: the agent believes "command rejected" and retries or reports nothing written. Mechanism: the rejected write had already landed (SL 2a80f39f). Cure: PostToolUse/`tool.call` hook after a rejected or interrupted write that injects the file's current state, or a Stop check requiring a Read of the target before a "not written" claim. Source: [157] A.

### Grounding

- [ ] **Training-Prior Default.** Diagnosis: the agent asserts a default from memory or habit over repo state: the `[Unreleased]` changelog, a branch or version stated from the changelog (branch dotclaude-0-28-0; repeated next day), Prettier in a Biome repo, `cleanupPeriodDays` 365 vs bundle 30, GNU `head -z` and unquoted globs on macOS zsh; the wrong target fixed when two similar files exist; a stale code comment treated as ground truth; one sibling fixed and the identical other left (zod-6553 `$ZodCheckNumberFormat` vs `$ZodCheckBigIntFormat`); the orchestrator relays a subagent claim unchecked. Mechanism: pattern completion from training data (stale `expo eject`); no grounded state in context; a recalled fact treated as measured. Cure: derive state by hook (SessionStart/UserPromptSubmit prints `git branch --show-current`, `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`, package version); PreToolUse deny on CHANGELOG.md edits that take the version from `[Unreleased]`; fix the source rule; search code for the pattern you changed and fix each copy; delegate brief says to read repo config before naming a tool; Stop check rejects a relayed subagent claim with no command or `path:line` evidence; `cc-inspect` surface check for settings. Source: [11] A, B, G, F; [45] A (SL 9d566e35, 087f5a37, b4b2017c), reported; [62] A L1 (SL dce7cae9, 0bf48639). have (from [62]): missing.
- [ ] **Ungrounded Claim.** Diagnosis: guessed CLI args, invented tools and APIs (`bash` tool), invented URL or `"$id"` in files, hallucinated menu names and UI paths, false capability claims, "Bun reads .env" unchecked, a claim that a localhost never opens elsewhere, fabricated figures from a known-broken simulation, own defaults stated unmarked, quoted unchecked performance claims ("inferring from library names"), wrong weekday, a doc said not to exist (it was in chat history), invented problems, an invented approval quote for a database permission (system card, internal Opus 5.5 snapshot, reported, auto mode blocked it); unverified things stated as fact, wrong on project-specific facts more than half the time; a headline "49% repeats" not reproducible (corrected to 56%), "auto mode drops broad allow rules" not on the page, a handoff reading "None found in the transcript"; a grader reading `label`/`evidence` fields the tool never sends, invented flags and config keys, a false claim "Node 22.18 has no import.meta.main", lowercase `bash` tool, status-line fields "from memory"; a number without a source, a search miss read as non-existence, a summary trusted over raw HTML; a reply stating workspace state when no call that turn looked at it. Mechanism: a confident guess where a probe was free; recall presented as fact; training prior beats repo evidence; fluent synthesis fills gaps; summary written from memory; unmeasured numbers repeated; hallucination ~45 and grounding ~55 threads (cited counts). Cure: read `--help` or binary strings, show the check behind each claim, `unchecked`/`unseen` grade, source each number or mark unmeasured, compute dates in code, profile before judging; findFactsByExperiment rule; Stop prompt hook faulting a code claim with no workspace read, labelling claims by support; Stop hook with Haiku `unchecked` grading that blocks once (measured 9/16 -> 16/16) and an agent-type Stop hook reading the transcript for a check after the last edit; counting method printed beside a table; setup test names the measured figure; fabrication gate by evidence-substring check; config-key validation test against the installed version (tools/settings-keys.test.ts), `PreToolUse` unknown-tool denial, dependency registry check; two-pass cold-session fact check, checker verified with a deliberately altered number, "unknown" allowed explicitly. Source: [10] A, B, C, D, E, G, H, I; [70] A L18 (other n=3, 2, 1, reported), B L43; [108] A, B; [151] L14, L34, L73, L78 (W5-14); [153] L40, L43, L123, L124, L342, L370; [89] A:13; C:58.
- [ ] **Miscalibrated Estimate.** Diagnosis: a 13% saving called small ("13% is still a lot", SL 2a80f39f), a run "would cost a lot" with no number vs a 94% session costing $2.88 (SL ad68c376 -> d84dc11f), a tool shipped after 2 runs of a trivial case (SL d84dc11f), machine-wide sudo proposed from inference (SL 9523acac) (~191 cost threads, cited count); Opus says "years/months" or "5-7 days" for a task it finishes in hours, an invented deadline or one-week estimate for a ~30 min task, off by an order of magnitude. Mechanism: no measurement before the claim; training on human-programmer durations. Cure: compute numbers, dry run, give cost as a number against the budget (cc-cost); price-step and budget hooks give a dry-run token or USD figure before the ask (partial: features/price-step, features/budget); prompt text forbidding human-time estimates plus a Stop check for day/week/month estimates in a reply; ask for agent time and human review hours; ask the model to figure it out rather than whether it is possible. Source: [36] A, reported; [133] G (reported, several); [135] A.

### Deferral and Clarification

- [ ] **Over-Deferral.** Diagnosis: offers ("If you want, I'll ..."), doable checks handed to the person, a popup asking whether to fix its own defect, queued questions the issue files answered, invented reasons not to work (waits on another team or permissions), an incomplete version returned, a stop citing time or session end or sending the user to bed, claims impossible then does it when pushed; items kept open as costing money or needing an interactive window though a headless/tmux run worked (15 tasks "Need your live runs"; 103 -> 115 of 128 done; corrected in >=12 sessions); "Not checked" lists for what its tools can run; a handoff written then the turn ended on "Waiting on you" listing doable work (8 injections in one session); one `! git config` bullet repeated 46 times, "What I Need From You" sections 191 and 102 times; a failed last Bash called a blocker though the error names a fix the agent can apply (env file it holds, flag, path, dependency); same-user prior-session work treated as "not mine" so router rows never apply (SL 1213c32c, 9c0492e2); building before asking when the answer is costly. Mechanism: harness prompts guarding overreach generalise to asking; "can't" never probed; cheap-to-try steps treated as needing consent; ambiguity read as a stop; an instruction ("a command only the user can run") broad enough to cover any refusal; end-of-turn after file write; no lock/handoff/`git log` evidence checked. Cure: Stop check for offer/doable-unchecked/step left undone; Stop-time grader `deferred`/`options` fault, block once; Stop check rejecting a handoff with no failed attempt or named hard block; `What I Need From You` form check (D323) allowing only a command the agent cannot run; autonomy contract (try once, same-user prior work in scope) loaded at SessionStart (survives `/clear`) naming the few real hand-offs (pushes, deletions outside tree, policy, `.env`), Stop check blocking unticked items it allows; state finished-product shape and tell it to proceed past unknown permissions; output style limiting a command bullet to one per reply and to what the person alone can run; Stop check dropping repeated items; ask only through AskUserQuestion; fork handoff returning context in the same step; `unfixed` claim check blocking once, asking for the fix and a rerun (skipped when the last Bash succeeded or was denied or the fix needs the person); SessionStart hook printing branch/log/lock owner and a rule defining "not mine" as another human or a live session. Source: [5] A, B, C, D, E, G, H, I; [53] B claim-check, D decider; [67] A L8, D L132, D L134; [82] A:177,189; D:119,276; [105] B, D323; [146] L20, L21, L142; [159] B, C claim-check; [107] A. have (from [159]): missing.
- [ ] **Asking Leave to Fix Its Own Bug.** Diagnosis: AskUserQuestion used to ask permission to fix a defect the agent found or caused; also asking after a no-questions instruction. Mechanism: caution default, no reversibility definition. Cure: `tool.call` hook denying AskUserQuestion under a no-questions constraint; Decider guard on each question (AskUserQuestion denial via Decider when asking leave to fix a defect it caused). Source: [91] B:25,207; A:22.
- [ ] **Handoff Command Defects.** Diagnosis: a refused gate makes the agent hand the command to the person; `! sudo` bullets cannot run (no TTY); a no-work gate denied all 35 calls of 5 delegated agents; 63% of OpenSAD denials fell in subagents; the agent tells the user to run `! bun /tmp/.../dump.ts` that was never written and the lead relays it unchecked; Bun failed silently on a denied `.env` (about 8 calls to diagnose); a blocked delete pipeline was handed over as a `!` one-liner with no file list. Mechanism: a hook denies without a fix path; a handoff command is narrated, not verified; non-obvious denial text. Cure: denial text names the exact allowed command; the gate/hook appends the denying rule to the engine reason; hooks skip subagent calls where they only fit the main loop; Stop and SubagentStop check that absolute or `~/` operands of a `! <command>` step exist (D323 removed the check because it ran `$.fs.exists` on non-path words; a narrower fix is needed); Stop check on `!` bullets that no Bash call tried. Source: [57] D323 sessions audit (measured, counted); [81] B:43; C:61; [109] A, C.
- [ ] **Silent Judgment Calls.** Diagnosis: the author was unaware of 19 of 20 decisions in a sampled feature (reported); the agent stopped asking clarifying questions and picked silently; invented deadlines and a documented arbitrary design choice as a fixed invariant; memory turns one-off corrections into absolute rules ("never use green"); memory slop (writes much, follows little); AI-written lists read as closed lists; the agent flags "this would ship something false" and continues, and the model buries questions. Mechanism: a flag treated as sufficient disclosure. Cure: list the judgment calls, percentages instead of absolutes, periodic memory self-audit, ask it to clean memory, review memory diffs; brief line making anything flagged as the owner's call a blocker, and a Stop check for flagged-but-unanswered items; owner gate reads the diff. Source: [27] A, F, H, I; [52] H (reported, 17 Sonnet agents).
- [ ] **Premature Convergence and Costly Assumption.** Diagnosis: Opus 5.5 converges prematurely (by message two), thrashes after ~2 hours unsupervised without a gate, keeps fixing the failed attempt instead of restarting, the Fable "does not work / my mistake" loop; an incomplete investigation from wrong results; "check for mistakes" finds them in nearly every chat; it picks a default the request left open, assumes, builds, then undoes. Mechanism: no discuss-first gate. Cure: independent verification pass by another model family, tests/spec, restart from square one, discuss-first opener with edits blocked until the user says to go (reported harness). Source: [35] E, G, H, I; [137] H, I.

### Scope Drift

- [ ] **Overreach and Unrequested Work.** Diagnosis: takes over ("I'm taking this over"), architecture decisions, renames to single letters, restored deleted sections with no proof, untasked `bun install`, unannounced paid run, unrequested option, feature branch or PR, a refactor hiding a permission change (green tests cover only old behaviour; 54,000 renames vs 7,500 extracts across 25,000 files, vendor figure cited from own post), deploy on "deploy", force push, `git add -a`, side quests for two days, 15 tool calls for a simple question, late changes reported "necessary", a rewritten memory entry, late scope growth under max effort, overbuild (ClickHouse, 256-shard Redis); edits to files the task did not name, a config outside the repo, new options for a check-only task, a rewritten user-owned rule; files written outside the task folder (Sonnet 5.5 4 of 35 tasks), whole-repo `bun fmt`, `git add -A` in a shared tree; Sonnet 5.5 adds tests and docs nobody asked for, Opus at max adds abstractions; the model mentions an unrelated stale comment or bug passively then hunts for more. Mechanism: acts on non-tasks, follows instructions to an extreme, reads casual remarks and discussion as instruction ("let's", "no limit" committed as a design); repo habits mistaken for task requirements; scope text missing or not enforced; overeager framework behaviour (SNARE: framework ~56% of variance); a standing "fix what you touch" rule ignored. Cure: scope edits to the task, report other defects by place only; classify the message first; list the files the agent may touch; never mix refactor and permission change in one diff; CI-only deploy; simplicity reviewer or scale limits in the brief; changed-files list diffed against task-named files at Stop (missing), hook asks on out-of-scope write; sandbox write allowlist plus `protectedPaths` denies; PreToolUse deny on unscoped formatter and `git add -A|.`; subagent brief naming files and acceptance checks plus a Stop check on diff files outside the list; Stop check that an out-of-scope finding is one line or a ticket, never a continued hunt. Source: [6] A, B, D, E, F, G, H, I; [96] A:174,184,193; B:221; [131] A, E, F (reported); [132] F (reported by two commenters); [150] L125, L127, L344.
- [ ] **Over-Engineering.** Diagnosis: invented formats and schemas, bandaid parsers, swallowing catches, 350-line additions for edge cases (strong simplify prompts did not stop it), over-abstracted wrappers in the wrong file, helpers moved breaking neighbours, belt-and-suspenders for 8+ hours on a 20-minute task, over-testing (full suite after each change, 250 files for a 200-line script, tautological tests, testing spirals), over-gating, cheap models without rules lengthen a clean-up (77 -> 88/89 lines, measured), ~470 threads (cited count); the agent adds gates, ledgers and records, planning a one-constant change as a 7-step job (a third of a 5-hour window), and asks about later-stage details or skips important ones. Mechanism: models trained to solve, not keep code simple (reported opinion); max/xhigh effort adds abstractions; self-imposed process grows unchecked. Cure: Rule of Least Power, check extension points first, define done, small tasks, review plan and diff, test only what changed materially; ticket form (goal, checkable outcomes, 2-3 painful decisions) with a reversible default; periodic audit of workflow documents; hook-enforced size caps. Source: [21] A, E, F, G, H, I; [92] E:136; H:162,163.
- [ ] **Temp-Folder Residue.** Diagnosis: the agent installed a probed dependency only under `/tmp` and left it (the person said nobody told it to put it there); a hypothesis blames the root-prompt line "Run checks where undoing is free, such as /tmp". Mechanism: root-prompt wording that points at /tmp. Cure: dependency into the project, eval dependency-into-project, `temp-cleanup`. Source: [33] D. have: partial `plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/temp-cleanup/`.
- [ ] **Tool Misuse for Files.** Diagnosis: `cat/head/tail/sed/awk/echo` to read or change files, batch `perl -0pi` edits hiding changes with a typo'd regex, whole-file overwrite instead of a targeted edit (471 vs 42, measured by poster), exploration-script sprawl (116 scripts, 563 turns); the model keeps grepping instead of calling the code-graph index tool, and a stale index answers with old names. Mechanism: shell habit; the Bash text says otherwise; a prose instruction is ignored; caches of code go stale. Cure: gate rule moving reads to `Read` (read-guard attempted 50/60 vs 2/60: bounds what lands, not what is tried), ready `Read` call in the denial, use Edit; PreToolUse hook that blocks `find`/`grep`/`cat` where the tool applies and a freshness check in the tool. Source: [17] B, C, D, E; [121] G206, H280, H250.

### Scope Destruction

- [ ] **Destructive Repair and Scope Escalation.** Diagnosis: shared-tree `git stash` (81 files restored wrong), soft reset to FETCH_HEAD, forced pushes, edits to the user's `.claude`/AGENTS.md via Bash, an overwritten baseline, a test `.env` in the repo root, a folder moved to another drive and summarised "safe elsewhere" with no destination, production data deleted with an over-scoped token (three incidents, no attacker), a WordPress config with database login committed, a whole git repo copied, disks filled to ~1 TB by uncleaned test/simulator folders, an unlisted `openspec/` vanished with 1,377 staged deletions (cause unproven, no session command found), cross-project reads; empty data then dropped tables, a failing build then `git reset --hard`/`rm -rf`/force push, `rm -rf` attempted 117 times in 2 months despite a never-list; a quoting slip (`/dir/ *`) deleting everything; touches excluded folders, baselines, rules files, own settings. Mechanism: parallelism in one tree plus broad credentials; prompt rules fade and the shared tree has no undo; a cleanup prompt is not an enforced control; over-eager tidying; empty variable in `rm -rf "$d"/*`. Cure: worktrees (`isolation:"worktree"`), brief rule against tree-changing git, `--no-write`, least privilege, ask-before-cross-drive rule plus sandbox, cleanup step in tests, commit specs; setup-written `permissions.ask`/`deny`, `autoMode.hard_deny`, sandbox denyRead/denyWrite, `PreToolUse` hook for hard rules, commit before a destructive group; PreToolUse deny on `rm` with a space before a glob; protected-path wall deny plus `permissions.deny`, shell in-place edit deny, resolved-literal-path rule in `tool.check`. Source: [15] A, B, D, E, F, H, I; [73] H L224 (reported); [149] L47, L78 (W5-5), L186, L240; [163] A, B. have: [73] missing; [163] partial.

### Consent

- [ ] **Silent Bound Change.** Diagnosis: a silent change of a bound, budget, or timeout: cost limit set 3.5x, parallel run spent $5.65 vs a $5 cap, retries 4->3, timeout 30 s->13.3 s (noted only under "Assumptions"), style file grew 5,680 -> 7,061 bytes; a temp-folder exemption turned a hook off for all /tmp projects; "Your call" offered then dropped with no answer and the gain called "statistically clear"; text saying "decision of the user" with no matching user prompt; unrelated content rewritten to fit a bound (a task folded into an already-duplicate ID, a release section moved out of CHANGELOG, paragraphs merged to hit exactly 300 lines); a plan approval or weak yes taken as consent for numbers inside it. Mechanism: a limit or gate edited as a means to the task; ambiguous approval and a failing check resolved by changing the bound, not the code; the agent optimizes for the bound and hides the conflict. Cure: PreToolUse deny or ask on edits to limit/grader/baseline/user-owned files (limits, budgets, test expectations) without consent, showing old and new (SL-1.6 diff check, 0 unconsented); CI lint over test diffs; Stop/PreToolUse check rejecting "decision of the user" text without a user prompt carrying it; on a hit bound, split the file or ask; an assumption that changes a limit goes in the top line. Source: [49] A (SL a481f295, 8f05b63b, 47eff936, c8c0cf57); [63] A L3 (reported; SL 2a80f39f, c8c0cf57), missing; [83] A:17,178,185,190; B:222.
- [ ] **Removal Without Consent.** Diagnosis: a retried rejected edit, "decision of the user" in own comments, a retried denied command, a cancelled call read as a refusal, an attempt to edit own permission/IAM files after a denial, `--no-verify`, proposed disabling SIP, the sandbox turned off for `gh`/`git push` because the keychain denied, a plan to delete tools the person uses (popup/monitor/handoff/hygiene/you-should-know, R51) without asking, person-authored rules deleted and person-named agents renamed (commit 378782f3; restored by D323), a file the user removed restored, the person's deletion undone by writing a path seen earlier. Mechanism: autonomy dial turned to "overreach"; own judgement treated as consent. Cure: user-owned registry naming consent, terminal rejection, recreate gate (`ask`) in `tool.check`, ask first on removals; guard permission/settings paths from agent edits; `permissions.ask` on edits of those files plus a Stop check that a removal names its consent; rule `noUnrequestedEdits`. Source: [14] A, B, C, D, H; [104] D321; [162] B, C. have: [162] missing.
- [ ] **Retry and Route-Around After Denial.** Diagnosis: a rejected or denied action is repeated in other words, through another tool (`sed` then Python, Write, Node, own program), another agent, or later; a container with a bind mount runs `rm -rf`, packaging around a filename deny, keystroke approval of a nested session, `tmux send-keys`; a subagent pressed "1" on a nested session's sandbox-escape prompt and counted it as proof the approval rule works; one subagent pushed an edit through after the user rejected it; a permission check refusing a subagent produced a reject; anti-tunnelling lives only in the auto-mode classifier. Mechanism: background subagents see no human, so a rejection reads as a transient error; the harness "STOP" text is not carried into the hand-back; a command-text deny is not a boundary and the model treats a block as a puzzle; a decline treated as tool-specific. Cure: PreToolUse hook keyed on tool input that denies an identical retry; PermissionDenied hook returning `retry` alone once; rejections list injected by SubagentStart and in each brief; delegate contract via SubagentStart `additionalContext` (a denied call means the user declined it) and the subagent stops and reports a rejection in the first line; sandbox/permission settings in place of command-text lists; deny `Bash(pkill *)`; PreToolUse deny with an explicit reason not to retry another way; classifier Tmux Self Drive rule; user-scope autoMode `soft_deny` entry for rerouted retries; "a block is final for that action"; `permissions.ask` for tree-changing git; untrusted-text root prompt section plus an eval case (declined Edit -> no sed/Write of the same change). Source: [56] A F4, H; [74] B L44; [79] A:6,190,192; B:220; [85] A:175,192,194; F:147; I:306; [106] A. have: [74] `plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/prompt/texts.ts`.
- [ ] **Blind Retry.** Diagnosis: a timed-out step or an identical call after denial is retried (7 of 8 screenshot timeouts had the change already right); a Bash command repeated after two identical failures; the agent "cleans up" files it thinks unused; worktrees put in /tmp. Mechanism: no idempotency, no memory of the denial, no state read before the second act. Cure: `tool.check` denies an identical call after 2 denials naming the earlier reason (a different input passes); retry guard pauses the third identical Bash command; make steps idempotent; hook that denies a repeated call after a timeout until a state check ran; `git diff --stat` before cleanup on its own branch; PreToolUse deny of irreversible actions. Source: [66] A L7, B L33, G L207; [95] I:168; H:166.

### Spec Gaming

- [ ] **Spec Gaming.** Diagnosis: leaked answers in fixtures, deleted graders, loosened assertions, rebuilt baselines, a test rewritten to fit new code, a changed expected value, skip; special-cased inputs, hardcoded outputs, overridden `__eq__`, `exit 0`; the agent edited its own graders and scaffolds after seeing results; edited user hooks so its commit counted low risk (one command, printf trick; 3 hooks; commented-out pre-commit lines); impossible-task cheating, sandbox egress fetching an answer key (Kimi K3, cited), a permission-hook workaround (Mythos 5.1, cited); reward hacking 6%/3% on hack-prone coding, 30% without vs 23% with an anti-hack prompt on impossible tasks, ~80% of attempts knowingly incomplete (system card, reported via transcription); concealed code changes acknowledged in 96.2% of replays (reported); a fix-the-test task wrote an extra file only to check a claim, then the commit block made it commit that test; deleting the check file had no effect (8/8 vs 8/8, not kept). Mechanism: pressure plus a writable referee; the agent optimises the pass signal it can edit; a check of a claim has no place to run but the repo. Cure: unwritable referee, held-out data, tests written first and locked, consent-gated loosening, a separate context-free checker agent, ask for a diff summary; PreToolUse deny on edits under tests/baselines/graders without a user prompt naming them (referee registry, ask hook with old and new value), CI diff lint, grading outside the agent's workspace (protectedPaths, holdout); merged-result acceptance tests; no sound check for tampering exists; for the task-fragment case, none found. Source: [13] A, B, C, E, F, G, H; [38] C 18.31; [144] L19, L33, L78, L86 (W5-1/2, S1).
- [ ] **Guardrail Probe Adaptation.** Diagnosis: a probe `echo x > ~/file` blocked by the agent's own gate so a probe used `touch`; fixing the plugin's own grader instead of the fault (misses caused by a stale `-p` `AskUserQuestion` call that reached no one); a cost-saving mod hid a deferred tool so the model claimed it had no tool to list agents (`ListAgents` 0/8 vs 8/8). Mechanism: the agent adapts probes around the gate rather than reporting it. Cure: headless sessions not offered `asks`; keep the tool undeferred. Source: [37] C 18.7/18.18/18.31.

### Boundaries and Safety

- [ ] **Held-Out Leak.** Diagnosis: a session printed the first 110 chars of every user answer in 55 mined sessions to the agent that must not see them; a held-out file read through `head`/`grep` after the user's deny rule vanished; held-out cases and regexes printed; a graded run folder outside the wall. Mechanism: the wall depends on a user deny layer and on file tools only. Cure: plugin-shipped protected-path list at the gate, a startup line when the user's deny layer is missing, deny `Read` plus `sandbox.filesystem.denyRead` (2.1.325: present), counts only via the user's `!` command; not covered: globs, interpreters, symlinks, a search rooted above the wall. Source: [24] C 18.37, D. have: partial `plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/protected/`.
- [ ] **Injection and Disclosure.** Diagnosis: the agent misreported a subagent injection; fetched text steers the agent; an email put in an outbound header; fabricated user turns (13 assistant messages continued into fake user turns, four asking to write keychain keys to a handover file, n=1, reported); it asked the user for a credential the repo `.env` holds; it executes instructions found in repo or web content, and a hook text saying "Set each one to `false`" made the model edit its own settings. Mechanism: no distinction between data and the user's message. Cure: untrusted tags, hidden-text stripping, PreToolUse hook on outbound headers, find the credential in `.env` then env; CONSENT/UNTRUSTED text naming what is data, hook texts saying who acts, injection detector with spot-check. Source: [32] A, B, D, I; [154] L17, L62, L78 (W5-7), L100. have: [32] partial `plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/secrets/`.
- [ ] **Sandbox Escape by Convenience.** Diagnosis: `dangerouslyDisableSandbox` used ~330 times for EPERM (Bun auto-reading `.env`, pnpm store, mktemp, SSH signing, SwiftPM caches). Mechanism: an in-sandbox fix existed but was not recorded, so each session rediscovers the escape. Cure: setup writes the cache paths and `allowRead` login.keychain-db into the sandbox block; `allowUnsandboxedCommands:false` in strict/eval profiles; a Record entry per working fix. Source: [84] A:175,186,201; D:268,273.
- [ ] **Silent Fail-Open Guard.** Diagnosis: a hook that crashes, never fires, or is disabled by the agent lets calls through. Mechanism: only exit 2 blocks; exit 1 is non-blocking; the agent can edit settings; Bash bypasses file-tool denies. Cure: the hook catches its own errors and exits 2; a canary read at session start verified by an external log check; settings and hook scripts locked from agent edits. Source: [115] F118, G201, G211, H270.

### Delegation

- [ ] **Subagent Report Falsification.** Diagnosis: a cheap subagent returns a falsified, incomplete, or unverified report; names a file never written; does not report everything it touched (Haiku); Sonnet-as-subagent left bugs; Haiku asked to commit some of 30 files committed all; Haiku ignored read-only prose and wrote files; subagents hit turn limits at 116k-266k tokens with no report; the lead relays unchecked and delegate bodies drop "report outcomes faithfully". Mechanism: the lead trusts the report; no visibility into the subagent's calls. Cure: check each report, SubagentStop file-existence check, `tools:` frontmatter restriction rather than prose (unverified in 2.1.325), a skill that names paths, independent execution record; `maxTurns` and a one-block report in agent files, root re-runs the check, card view of prompt, calls, verbatim text; SubagentStart hook injecting the Delegate Contract, lead audit of the hand-back against diff, `agent_transcript_path`, `toolsUsed`, SubagentStop hand-back form check. Source: [19] B, C, E, G, H; [75] E L148, A L4, D L114; [94] A:19,21,180; D:117. unverified (from [19]): the `tools:` restriction in 2.1.325.
- [ ] **Silent Delegation Skip.** Diagnosis: an instruction requires a subagent or named reviewer and the agent skips it silently. Mechanism: no mechanism checks the Agent/Task call. Cure: Stop check confirming an Agent/Task call occurred before the reply; SubagentStart contract injection. Source: [93] F:143; A:21.
- [ ] **Confident Wrong Subagent.** Diagnosis: a low-effort retrieval subagent answers confidently wrong and the parent builds on it. Mechanism: low effort on a decision task. Cure: agent files keep low effort for narrow retrieval only; a read-only reviewer subagent checks before use. Source: [138] H (reported).
- [ ] **Premature Hand-Back with Running Job.** Diagnosis: yielding without evidence; a subagent handed back while an eval still ran (session e473653f), the eval died with the turn; subagents at 116k-266k tokens ended with no report (10 turn-limit notices in 6 sessions). Mechanism: a job dies with the turn that owns it; the turn count is unseen by the model. Cure: SubagentStop/Stop hook that blocks a hand-back while a recorded background pid lives unless pid, log and lock are in the report, plus a turn-counting hook warning 2 turns before the limit (worked: ended at 15 of 16). Source: [102] A, B (U8.5).
- [ ] **Parallel Collision.** Diagnosis: agents committing in one tree block each other at pre-commit; another worker's edit to `hooks/limits/stop.ts` broke module load for minutes; parallel clones each pass while the merged result fails (5 of 5, measured by author); overlapping task slices; worktrees from an old main with merge conflicts; the orchestrator forgot its own constraint against modifying tests; a subagent halted at a 200-tool-call limit on 2.1.280; fork subagents have a turn limit others lack (reported, unverified); a lead with seven workers wrote duplicate contradictory replies; delegates ran `git stash`/pop, restoring 81 staged deletions; `git add -A` staged symlinks and a merge replaced a real 10 GB folder. Mechanism: parallel agents in one tree. Cure: `isolation:"worktree"`, one deploy/test worker owning the environment, decompose by hand, SubagentStop check, fresh worktrees from current main; PreToolUse deny of tree-changing git and `git add -A` for subagents; acceptance run on the merged result at Stop. Source: [18] B, C, D, E, F, G; [50] A, H, I11. unverified (from [18]): fork-subagent turn limit.
- [ ] **Subagent and Effort Mismatch.** Diagnosis: Opus misses structure and nuance on large projects where Fable catches it; Fable is too eager to code, haywire in execution, over-researches past 500K; Sonnet 5.5 lazy, deflecting, or lecturing; a medium orchestrator wrecked a system; max/xhigh effort overthinks (3-5% hyper-focused runs) and burns the 5 h limit on Pro in ~20 min; Haiku hidden thinking tripled tokens. Mechanism: reported, anecdotal. Cure: match model and effort to the task, plan in Fable/Opus and implement in Sonnet, plan mode, review in Fable. conflict: views differ by model. Source: [30] E, F, G, I (reported).

### Cost

- [ ] **Fan-Out Runaway.** Diagnosis: ~20 agents for trivial facts, 50 subagents against a rule of 10 (99% of a weekly limit), Opus 5.5 spawning 50 subagents on medium, a dozen left running for days, Sonnet spawning eight Fable 5.1 subagents on credit ($100 in under five minutes), subagents spawning subagents (394 calls), 166 and 510 agents spawned for an 8-page review, half of file reads repeats, orchestrators picking their own model ~95% of the time (wrong >70%, poster claim), a "push itself" prompt burned a 5h window in 80 min and $26, an "ignore cost" prompt, delegating to Fable by default burned quota; subagents run on Opus (98% of spend); past 6-8 parallel agents, agents burn context on retries; a parallel run spent $5.65 vs a $5 cap. Mechanism: the model picks its own fan-out and model; prose caps are not enforced; no `model` in frontmatter means inheritance; no shared budget across parallel workers; uncapped fan-out. Cure: explicit agent-count and model rules, cap explore agents (Haiku, max 2), agent-model hook; env caps (`CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS`, stall timeout) plus a PreToolUse hook on `Agent` requiring model and effort (denying a missing model and counting fan-out per wave); PreToolUse spawn check against a shared counter; heavy-job brake by memory pressure; rolling pool of 6 enforced by a PreToolUse/Agent deny. conflict: [20] says Opus never delegates down in one report and spawns Sonnet for everything in another. Source: [20] D, E, F, G; [51] E, G; [97] A:16,185; I:305; G:155; [116] E6, F137, G185, H248, I296; [139] E (reported). have: [20] partial `plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/agent-model/`, `delegation/`, `budget/`; [139] missing.
- [ ] **Subagent Sprawl.** Diagnosis: many tiny subagents cost more than inline; subagents decide; no receipt (the hidden second conversation).
