### Goes

- [ ] **Dogma on compaction ("never compact" or "always compact").** Evidence is mixed (compact keeps user messages and is cheaper per event vs generic, lossy, session-tied); drop dogma, keep the measurable rule under Keep. Includes endless compaction as default, `/compact` as main memory strategy, handoff prompt or `/compact` asked of a 900k session (pays the full cold read; use a notes file kept current), and a month-long single session (poisoned context, cold-cache cost). Sources: E61, F150, F151, F156, F158. [reported, contested]
- [ ] **Auto-compact at the default ~967K for cost-sensitive users, and dislike of auto-compact.** Replaced by window tuning or handoff at 150-250k with a toggle (see Keep); turning auto-compact off to shrink context is false: the 85k-to-38k saving was a reserved buffer shown as used (reported both ways, unresolved). Sources: I294, G215, F154. [reported]
- [ ] **Replacing the system prompt wholesale (`--system-prompt` with a community-extracted prompt).** Risks losing subagent exploration and tool guidance; prompt copy goes stale with daily releases; project rule: never patch the binary. Flags `--system-prompt` (replace) and `--append-system-prompt` (add) exist as separate flags (see Facts). Source: E62. [reported]
- [ ] **Rewriting CLAUDE.md on each model version; keeping old code versions in CLAUDE.md; loading a whole memory file every message; generated repo-description from `/init`; giant instruction file and thousands of lines of skills.** Keep only repo-specific burn-list rules (package manager, never start the server, ask before migrations, no surprise subagents); move the rest to hooks and on-demand skills. Sources: E63, E65, F159, G216, E75. [reported]
- [ ] **Terse-output / caveman skills as the token fix, and thank-you-costs-tokens noise.** Context and tool-call optimisation matter more than cutting words (~33%). Sources: E64, F153. [reported; weak]
- [ ] **Third-party compaction, memory, and token tools (supercompact, Squeez, ClawMetry, md2, tokendoctor, shared-memory services, cache-audit skill, plugin promos).** Single-poster promos with no measurements; cache-audit reduces to "optimise startup context". Sources: E66, E69, E70, E72, E73, F161(related), H283. [reported, inferred]
- [ ] **Context-management plugins that prune tool output or messages, and compression/economizer plugins.** Net loss on a prefix cache; preserve-thinking invalidates thinking blocks, giving amnesia about reasoning. Sources: E68, E71. [reported]
- [ ] **`/export` pasted into a fresh session as handoff; hand-written handoff for "working style"; generic piling of more instructions on drift.** Pasted history refills the context; working style does not transfer by prose; more instructions worsened drift, so change what rules are made of (provenance, enforcement). Sources: F155, H263, H262. [reported]
- [ ] **Haiku 5.5 as main coding agent; Fable as orchestrator for everything.** Face-plants on real repos (keep to bounded, verifiable jobs); Fable spawned 5 agents for 1 agent's work (contested, others run it for months). Sources: F157, F152. [reported]
- [ ] **Auto memory without review; post-hoc output-stripping mod; unattended video-generation showcase (portable item: an explicit spend cap in the prompt); star-count authority; anti Claude-speak joke thread; claude.ai chat-length and onboarding posts; misc jokes.** Sources: F160, F161, E67, I295, E74, E72, E73, E75. [reported]

### Facts

- [ ] **Context-size anchors (all reported).** Degradation reported beyond ~128-256k; 200-250k begins to pollute, 300-400k avoid; ceilings ~250k Opus/Sonnet, ~400k Fable; 60% fill "ideal time" attributed to docs (not read); compaction triggers: manual below 400k, 250-300k for quality, auto at 350k with handoff before; 50-70% guard (orchestrator retired at 50%); ~55% closeout handoff; 500k workable where 100k used to be the limit; "1M window but only the first 200k accurate" (cited second-hand); sessions suggest handoff ~250k (rare); half of one author's token use came from turns above 200k. Sources: E88, E100, E101, E102, E106, H267, H271. 2.1.325: present (`/autocompact`, `autoCompactWindow`).
- [ ] **Compaction cost anecdotes (reported).** 950k Fable session: one `/compact` = 14% of 5h limit ($59.73); ~500k compact = 20% of a Max 5x window; >500k context burned 20% in 30 s; 255K thread reported 128K after a switch; 8-12 concurrent sessions reached 1.6B tokens/day on Fable 5.1; cache read dominates tokens (`claude-opus-5-5: 370.4m cache read, 4.0m cache write`). Sources: E88, E95, E96. 2.1.325: n/a.
- [ ] **Cache economics.** `/clear` costs zero tokens but loses context; compact costs a summariser run but keeps the prefix cached; cache expires after idle, compact before leaving >1h; reads ~0.1x, write 1.25x (5m) or 2x (1h); at 500k a read per turn ~50k base-input equivalent, compact to ~30k = one write ~37-60k; cache reads ~88% of tokens (author log), 95-97% (Pro, TTRPG; ~160 cache-read tokens per output token); Codex reviews ~90% from cache; idle ~1h10m forces full re-send; subscription main-conversation cache 1h; advisor calls uncached; 120 sessions: median peak ~371k, 17 sessions >500k used 34.4% of cache-read tokens; every turn resends the whole conversation (system prompt, tools, CLAUDE.md, messages, tool results). Sources: E89, F167, F168, F170, G223, H272, H275, H276. [reported, some measured by authors] 2.1.325: present (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`).
- [ ] **Starting-context numbers.** `/context` splits system prompt, system tools, MCP tools, memory files, custom agents; fresh start 10k-45k (80k baseline with plugins; 30-40k system prompt plus tools); autocompact buffer ~33k (reported: part of the window reserved). Sources: G225, G229, H266, H276. 2.1.325: present (`/context`).
- [ ] **CLAUDE.md and memory mechanics.** `#` appends to memory; `/memory` opens files; `@path` imports in CLAUDE.md; failed MCP servers are named with the real error at session start ("unavailable" is not "not configured"); `@include` reuses prompt files and does not replace the system prompt; CLAUDE.md under 200 lines, context not enforced (cited, memory docs); CLAUDE.md re-injected after compaction (reported by poster); best-practices page https://code.claude.com/docs/en/best-practices; long sessions with irrelevant context reduce performance; after two corrections on one issue the context holds failed approaches (cited, docs not re-read). Sources: E90, E91, E92, G221, G227, G228. 2.1.325: present (`/memory`; flags `--system-prompt`, `--append-system-prompt`).
- [ ] **Study and benchmark claims (cited second-hand, not verified).** ETH Zurich: LLM-generated context files reduced success vs none, ~20% more cost; human-written improved ~4%; Vercel cut 80% of tools, success 80% to 100%; 10 memory systems on BEAM 100K: top two 70.1%/67.8% vs full context 69.5%, BM25 55.3%, claude-mem 51.5%, CIs ~10 points (retrieval beat bulk context; layers cause anchoring; no repo action). Sources: E93, H284. [cited, unverified]
- [ ] **Compaction internals and records.** `getCompactPrompt` builds a dedicated user message; `getCacheSharingParams` reuses the main system prompt to hit cache (commenter reading source, unverified); `compact_boundary` JSONL records carry `compactMetadata.trigger`, `preTokens`, `postTokens`, `durationMs` (author-measured; unverified in surface); default window on a 1M model ~967K, summary ~76 s; handoff-compact needs 2.1.287+ with mods (cited); DIY compaction: summarise older turns with a cheaper model, keep last 5-10 turns, summary at top, keep decisions and open tasks; the API has server-side compaction (docs link not fetched); progress-bar stall at 95% guessed to be summary generation. Sources: E97, E105, H279, H281, I299. 2.1.325: unverified for the function names and JSONL fields.
- [ ] **Compaction messages and flags.** "Compacted while idle, before the prompt cache expired" (reported; only off via auto-compact off in the post, but 2.1.325 has `idleCompaction`: refutes the post's doubt); `/autocompact <n>` values 250k/272k/400k/450k ("will double your usage", unverified); an Anthropic-docs claim for 250k is unsourced. Sources: G218, E98, H268. 2.1.325: present (`idleCompaction`, `/autocompact`, `autoCompactWindow`, `--autocompact`, `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`).
- [ ] **Compaction text (observed).** The continuation instruction is the summary's last line in this session's summary format. Source: E103. [observed]
- [ ] **Keep-the-Why experiment.** 20 sessions on a retry-wrapper task: with a context folder 10 of 10 declined the rejected change, without 7 of 10 offered it; median 18 s vs 43 s. Source: E104. [measured by author, hand graded]
- [ ] **Subagent cache and cost mechanics.** Each subagent is fresh context (brief + tool defs + reads written to cache then re-read per turn); 88% of one panel's cost was cache writes; five agents started at the same instant each pay full write (inferred); Anthropic article of Oct 22 (cited via poster): default to one model/context/tool loop, procedures in skills, hard limits in tools, tool choice degrades past 30-50 definitions, Claude Code's own system prompt for newer models cut by over 80%, evals first (10 golden examples), failure modes lost context, sprawl, "Frankenprompt"; community: keep subagents for adversarial review by a different model, read-heavy disposable context, cheap parallel jobs. Sources: H265, H274. 2.1.325: present (`SubagentStart`, `SubagentStop`).
- [ ] **Haiku 5.5 pricing and use.** Lower rate under 100k request context on the API (not in the app plan); "40x cheaper under 100k"; light use (10 prompts) = 0% of the 5h window; chat re-sends the conversation so a long session crosses 100k silently; under 1% of one author's token use. Lineup named in a post (Oct 2026): Fable 5.1, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5 (post erroneously says Haiku 4.5); account models per repo rules. Sources: G222, G224, G226, F168. [reported]
- [ ] **Session behaviour reports.** Session stuck 15-45 min with zero tokens under 30% context is Claude Code waiting on the API (inferred; Esc, `/context`, `/compact`, then new session); desktop hid the context UI and reportedly has no auto-compact; terminating a session may not expire the cache (unverified, one user); Claude cannot read its own context percentage, a hook or script can (reported, unchecked); "nerf" explained by environment rot, A/B routing, infra bugs, harness changes, nerf trackers showed no degradation (cited by commenter). Sources: G219, H268, H269, F172. 2.1.325: unverified.
- [ ] **Mods, hooks, and versions.** Mods change what runs before a tool call, what the model sees and what appears on screen (cache timer, next-steps card, context monitor, delegation, session mirror); function-hook plugins early access on 2.1.289; auto-resume after a rate limit (CLI 2.1.234+); 2.1.322 `ip_reminder` block appeared in context; 2.1.323 (2026-11-12) experimental Code Mode (JS calls tools; one bad line kills a write batch per one team), plugin engines pinning, TS hook validate; summaries "unverified" on claim-by-claim basis. Sources: F171, G223, I300, E20. 2.1.325: unverified for `ip_reminder` and Code Mode wording; `claude plugin validate` unverified.
- [ ] **Cost distributions.** Session cost 0.19 to 50.68 USD over 10,000+ proxied requests; sessions grow context mainly in the first 5 hours (183k to 366k tokens per request, 139 sessions); fresh chat 0.038 vs 0.007-0.015 USD in a long chat (Pro, Sonnet), reload ~38k base tokens after `/clear`; 14-day measurement of 879 sessions: TDD-step-to-Opus rule cost 20% of spend, 35% repeat reads, security scan 26 s due to 11 leftover worktrees (678k files) vs 0.7 s after cleanup. Sources: I301, H277. [reported / measured]
- [ ] **Reported practice summary (mod-bot, 30 comments).** Skill files are suggestions not laws; short look-around on small repos, CLAUDE.md plus ADRs on large repos; treat context as a budget; Claude Code likes `/tmp` for worktrees and scratch (can be wiped on WSL restart or fill disk); tell it a session is ending so it writes notes; restart when you repeat a correction or the model revives a rejected idea; one task per session; context in files; edit the handoff yourself. Sources: H278, H282. [reported consensus]
- [ ] **Firecrawl and indexing.** Clean Markdown beats raw HTML for context use (reported); indexing a monorepo with codebase-memory-mcp took 1.5 s for ~9k nodes and ~21k edges (measured by poster); code-map skill stale-index lesson (see Keep). Sources: E94, G230, H280. [reported / measured]

## Cost and Models

Verification notes. `2.1.325` marks come from cc-inspect `surface`; `have/partial/missing` from read-only grep of `/Users/krystian/CodeProjects/xsyetopz/claudetto/plugins/`. Basis labels (measured, cited, reported, inferred) kept as input. Third-party prices for models outside claude-opus-5-5, claude-sonnet-5-5, claude-haiku-5-5, claude-fable-5-2, claude-mythos-5-2 are dropped as out of scope (Fable 5.1, Opus 5, Sonnet 5, GPT and Astra/Sol rows).

### Keep

#### Measure cost correctly
- [ ] **Billed cost per finished task is the only saving.** `if billed_cost_per_finished_task does not fall: report "no saving found"; revert the trim and record the reading`. Token or char count is not cost: the largest compression setup removed 38.4% of delivered tool-output tokens yet billed cost rose 6.8% (Pearson r 0.15) (arXiv 2607.12161, cited); turn count explains 84% of variance of log trajectory cost (arXiv 2610.15093, cited). Also measure cost per accepted change, including turns spent recovering context, so a lower re-read share does not hide rework. Sources: B specs/prompt-footprint, specs/surface-trim; E file 256; F re-read thread. have: missing (`billed cost` absent from plugins/). 2.1.325: n/a.
- [ ] **State a dry-run number when cost is the reason to ask.** `ask_text = "<tokens|USD> from dry run"`, never "would cost a lot": a guessed pessimistic figure was $2.88 for a 94% session (SL ad68c376 -> d84dc11f). Source: A. have: missing. Basis: reported by the dossier.
- [ ] **Count each response once.** `key = message.id + requestId; sum usage once per key`. One response spans several transcript lines with the same usage; naive sums overcount by more than 2x (output inflated 3.4x in one priced log); `--resume` and forks copy history so rows repeat. Any cost hook summing transcripts double-counts without it. Basis: reported/measured by one user. Sources: G 12-month log thread, 19,593-request pricing thread; F per-day report. have: missing (no `requestId`/`message.id` in plugins/; partial: plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/price-step/price-step.ts reads cache_read).
- [ ] **Account cache apart from reads.** `report = {cache_read, cache_write(5m), cache_write(1h), miss_rebuild_after_gap>TTL}`; optimise the miss, not the read. Measured by one user over 19,593 requests ($3,465, list prices): writes 3% of tokens, 46% of cost; another log: reads 96.5% of tokens, ~48% of cost, writes 3.3% tokens ~44% cost; misses 1-2% of requests but ~14% of cost, mostly prefix changes, not TTL expiry. A return after the cache lapsed: 35 returns in 27 days cost $98.74 vs $2.95 warm. Rebuild causes: gap past TTL, every subagent spawn (no shared cache), anything rewriting history (compaction, clearing old tool results, mid-session model switch). Same call priced $0.0183 then $0.0035 with equal tokens (cache state only). Price each response at its own model price, never fixed weights. Consequence: a nudge before idle-expiry or a handoff beats chasing "re-read" totals; measure tokens, not dollars. Sources: E file 238, G, F, I. have: missing (partial: compaction/handoff.ts 59-minute age rule).
- [ ] **Split `cache_creation` by TTL.** `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` vs `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` tells which TTL a session got; some Opus 5.5 sessions got 5-minute instead of 1-hour around 04:00 UTC (reported, one commenter, CLI 2.1.292). Weekly-bar jumps can be TTL, not volume. have: missing (`ephemeral_1h` absent).
- [ ] **Local usage numbers beat impressions.** `usage` is in `~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl`; ccusage or a small script gives tokens and dollars; replay own sessions at windows 200K-600K; read usage numbers, not message text; watch per-turn input, cache read/write and output, not only the quota (forks and uncached tool turns show there). One poster: 21B own tokens, 97 of 100 re-reads of content already read, so a cheaper model alone does not fix it; another: 3.4B tokens in 30 days, ~97% cache reads, so check the split before optimising; Claude Projects 113 threads 2.3B tokens with 97% hit rate, ~160 cache-read tokens per output token (reported). Arithmetic: cache reads = tool calls x average context, so ~20M per thread is not a bug. Sources: E, F, G, H, I. have: partial (plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/budget/budget-run.ts, price-step/price-step.ts).

Plans can rest on false premises. `/explain-usage` exists (2.1.325: present, prompt command). Basis: reported. Source: E. have: missing.
- [ ] **Cost audit categories.** Flag: old unrelated tasks riding along (50k+ tokens re-read per request), re-caching after pauses longer than cache life, premium model on read-only work, 3+ failed-tool loops, repeated Read of the same lines with an earlier copy in context, correction rate per project; each finding paired with a one-line fix (`/clear`, `/compact` with keep-list, read-only Sonnet subagent). Reported: about a fifth of one 2-day session's API-equivalent spend each on re-reading old work and on re-caching. Sources: G, I. have: missing.

#### Sessions and context
- [ ] **Session boundary by problem, not percent.** `switch when task changes or at a phase boundary; /clear between unrelated tasks; write handoff notes (goal, done, next, decisions) before the clear; next session starts from that file`. Keep 150-250k tokens (500k at most) and under ~150 turns; plan tasks below 150k. Cost is not linear in context: cache reads scale with everything in the thread (round 40 costs several times round 5 at the same 20% fill). One author priced six months of transcripts: 56% of spend was context re-reads after the cache discount, ~20% useful work, almost half of calls above 200k tokens (measured, own logs). Reported: auto-compact capped at 350-400k, ~30% more weekly use; a 250k-token cache miss ~18% of an hourly window on $20 plan. `/btw` for side questions, `/branch` to detach; a SESSION_BASE template at switch. Sources: E, F, G, H. have: partial (plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/compaction/handoff.ts: fork writes handoff at auto-compaction and at 59 minutes of age); percent/task-boundary trigger missing.
- [ ] **Never switch model mid-conversation.** A switch rereads the whole session uncached (opusplan then sonnet in one session was costly); cache is per model; `--model opusplan`/plan mode changes only top-level `model` (measured, CC 2.1.316); `/opusplan` called redundant now that subagent handoff works (unverified). Keep context under 500k even with 1M. 2.1.325: `opusplan` alias unverified (not in model list queried). Sources: B, E, F, G. have: missing.
- [ ] **Prefix-changing events (measured, CC 2.1.316, one version).** `/model` or plan mode under opusplan; MCP enable/disable with `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH=false` (2.1.325: present; first difference at tools[22]); first request after `/compact` (system[0] differs: cc_version suffix derives from first user message); CLI upgrade (system[0] billing header carries cc_version). Handover on 2.1.319 (fake API): first request byte-identical in system, tools, model, thinking, metadata, messages[0]; only messages[1] (engine environment block) differs. Source: B, C. have: n/a (knowledge).
- [ ] **Wrap-up allowance (reported, CC 2.1.277+ as posted).** When the 5-hour limit hits mid-response, a small weekly allowance lets Claude reach a stopping point; no direct quota access. Pair with auto wrap-up when pacing predicts a limit hit next turn. Source: E, F. have: missing.
- [ ] **Early-window trick (reported, unverified).** `send trivial message to cheap model at day start so the 5h reset lands mid-work`; a scheduled 7am task variant. have: missing.
- [ ] **Harness weight.** Harness matters as much as model: SNARE framework ~56% of variance in overeager behaviour vs model ~21% [2605.28122] (cited); harness alone 5-30x cost (catalog Cat 24:3 citing [2608.01347], multiplier is the catalog's). Split opinion on Opus 5.5: majority says heavy harnesses (Superpowers, GSD) waste tokens and a short grill-me skill, spec split into issues, a definition of done and a fresh-context adversarial review suffice; minority keeps design-implement-verify. Sequential execution is cheaper than per-step subagents; tell it to do only essential checks and verify all at the end. Hypothesis-first runs (n=1): 55 turns/300k tokens vs 563 turns/3.51M with 116 exploration scripts. Review which spec rules still earn their place (one reported a direct fix faster than a spec chain, measured once). Sources: A, E, F. have: partial (plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/surface/surface.ts trims unused skills and tools).
- [ ] **Tool surface cost.** Few, well-scoped tools; 50+ MCP tools ~72k tokens (reported); typed params, examples, actionable errors "X failed because Y; try Z"; idempotent retry-safe tools with 3-5 bounded retries, backoff, jitter, circuit breakers; test timeout, empty, malformed, partial. Audit skills, MCPs and agent files about every 3 months (reported). Source: A, H. have: partial.

#### Model and effort routing
- [ ] **Route by cost of a wrong answer (reported, contested).** `plan=strongest(Opus|Fable); implement=Sonnet|Opus; review=Opus|Fable; mechanical=Haiku|script`. Sonnet works with an explicit plan (exact files plus acceptance checks, one step at a time) and tidy code, fails on vague plans (invents names, shortcuts); Fable for big planning, security-sensitive review, unsticking loops, as read-only adversarial reviewer that never blesses; Opus 5.5 the preferred implementer; Haiku for mechanical predefined work, not intent. Counter: some pay for Opus rather than Opus cleaning up after Sonnet; Sonnet-implements/Opus-reviews cost more and took longer than Opus alone on both tasks of one measurement; thinker-planner-typist chains disputed (no consensus, measure per workload). Plan file must carry what execution needs (planning context is not minimal execution context). Orchestrator effort high with spawned agents low (one report). Sources: A, E, F, G, H, I. have: partial (plugins/claudetto/agents/{Artemis,Athena,Heracles,Metis}.md set model and effort; plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/agent-model/model.ts); blind-tester and read-only reviewer roles missing.
- [ ] **Agent files present.** Artemis `claude-haiku-5-5` effort high; Athena, Heracles, Metis `claude-sonnet-5-5` effort medium (read from agents/*.md). Note: repo instructions list `claude-fable-5-2` and `claude-mythos-5-2` as the models for Athena, yet Athena.md sets sonnet-5-5; a repo-side question, unresolved.
- [ ] **Orchestrator cap.** `pool=6; while queue: start agent when a slot frees`. Past 6-8 parallel agents one workload hit server-side rate limits and agents burned context on retries; an uncapped 19-agent Fable fan-out hit the session limit in ~45 min (reported). Spend the strongest model only where judgment decides (architecture, integration, review, merges); give mechanically verifiable work to Sonnet/Haiku. Sources: E. have: missing (partial: agent-model feature).
- [ ] **Subagent model.** `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=sonnet` (2.1.325: present, plus `_FORCE`); Opus per call for hard ones; escalate when Sonnet fails twice then Opus; subagent rule one-liners "evaluate tasks and farm them out to appropriate model subagents" credited with a lower plan tier (reported: 89% weekly by day 5 fell under 25%). Subagents use a 5-minute cache, main thread 1 hour on subscription (reported; but `subagentPromptCacheTtl` 2.1.325: present, repo writes `promptCacheTtl`/`subagentPromptCacheTtl` "1h"). Sonnet is ~95% of subagent messages, so Opus-vs-Sonnet differences are confounded with role. Sonnet or Haiku as reader/file scout so the main chat stays small. have: setup/settings.ts sets `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL: SONNET`.
- [ ] **Alias pinning.** setup writes `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_SONNET_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL` to full 5.5 IDs so default, opus, sonnet, haiku, opusplan and an agent's `model:` alias never resolve to an older model (observed without pin: picker showed "Default (recommended) Opus 5", `opusplan` named claude-opus-5). 2.1.325: present (all four). have: plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/setup/settings.ts:120-124. Source: B specs/tier-profiles.
- [ ] **Effort selection (Opus 5.5).** `default=medium; planning, unfamiliar scoping, edge-case bug fixes, review=high; xhigh only for the planning turn or architecture review; mechanical implementation from a step list, renames, small fixes=low|medium; max rarely`. Reason: thinking tokens are output tokens. Retry rule: medium with a redo at higher effort on failure beats xhigh/max throughout (same test fails twice -> escalate the stuck subtask). Orchestrator effort and subagent models can be named in the prompt. Low for narrow retrieval subagents, not for ones making codebase decisions (confident wrong answer the parent builds on). Ask Claude to pick model and effort for the next session when it writes the handover file; calibrate by rerunning 3-4 real tasks at medium and high; high means fewer check-ins with the user (docs reference, reported), lower effort for tighter control; max on implementation adds unrequested abstractions. Per-call cost example (one user): low ~0.45 USD, medium 0.85, high 2.35. Easy debug 1.5 min medium vs 9 min xhigh. Max ~6x medium usage (commenter). 2.1.325: `/effort` present, `--effort <level>` flag present. have: partial (agents carry `effort:`; no rule that picks effort by task, missing). Sources: A, E, F, G, H. Basis: reported/cited.
- [ ] **Effort in code review (measured by one poster, not agentic).** 36 seeded bugs, 144 `claude -p --effort` runs: recall 80% low, 85% medium, 86% high (noise ~4 pts, so medium equals high); false alarms per review 3.4, 2.3, 1.9; output tokens Opus 5.5 1,496/2,701/3,734, Haiku 5.5 5,930/10,993/18,614; Sonnet 5.5 low false alarms 5.8 vs 0.8 high. Practice: medium by default, high when a false alarm blocks a merge. Cross-lab review did not beat same-lab (22 of 100 defects caught by the other lab). Source: H. Fills the E gap "no benchmark of best effort for code review".
- [ ] **Advisor pattern.** `main works; strong model consulted before committing to an approach, when stuck, before done; sees full transcript`; set `advisorModel` (2.1.325: present) or `--settings '{"advisorModel":"opus"}'`; `/advisor` (2.1.325: present). Each call re-reads full history uncached (commenter citing docs, hypothesis): measured one user 95 calls, 19.6M uncached input tokens, 3-5% of usage; call count drives cost, so put the first call on committing to an approach while the change is small. Poster's final advice: Sonnet 5.5 high main with Opus advisor. Cited second-hand: Opus 5.5 plus Fable advisor 1.7 points over Opus alone for ~2.1x cost. Counter-view: Fable as orchestrator/judge, Opus as `/advisor fable` main at decision points; whether a Fable advisor draws a separate allowance is unverified. conflict: repo setup writes `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL: "1"` (2.1.325: present) so the advisor is off by default. have: partial (plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/setup/settings.ts:178 disables it; `advisorModel` absent).
- [ ] **Per-model notes (Table 6, cited from Anthropic 5.5 guides plus corpus).** Opus 5.5 stops prematurely in four named ways (announce next step, offer to continue, list non-blocking decisions); Sonnet 5.5 calls tools with wrong case (`bash`; 6 corpus calls, all subagent), adds unrequested tests/docs, may report done unchecked at low effort; Haiku 5.5 stops early and reports unverified at low/medium. Sonnet 5.5 batches reads into one shell command and writes whole files (fewer test runs 1.6 vs 6.7, passes 25/30 -> 30/30, ~$0.15 vs $0.36 per task; one poster, 10 tasks x 3 runs, saturated). Cost at max effort: Sonnet 5.5x its high, Opus 3.44x, Haiku 4.34x. Speed does not follow price (one 7-task set: Opus 5.5 ~121 s, Sonnet 5.5 ~140 s, Haiku 5.5 ~100 s, measured). Single game-dev anecdote: Opus 5.5 180k output tokens, all remaining 85% usage, unfinished; Sonnet 5.5 58k, ~15%, finished. Sonnet 5.5 as orchestrator argued less (single poster) vs an orchestrator that pushes back catches downstream errors. Source: A, E, G, H.
- [ ] **Safeguard workaround ladder (reported).** New session when an earlier part trips the classifier; clean personalization and old harness prompts; drop to Opus 4.8 for security-adjacent tooling then return (Opus 4.8 outside scope); ask another Claude why it hit. Opus 5.5 falls back to Opus 5 when safeguards trigger (poster). have: missing.
- [ ] **Classifier model by measurement.** Ship the built-in classifier with trimmed `autoMode` lists; a plugin `tool.check` route (Jev first, fallback claude-haiku-5-5) scored 29/30 vs built-in 30/30 on 30 cases (2026-11-06; stable miss on `push-unnamed-branch`). tool.check only tightens a verdict or turns a bare ask on a read-only command into allow; never ask a model to judge permission. `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_MODE_MODEL` does not change the model (server-configured model wins; 2.1.325: present as env but ineffective, measured); set it nowhere without a measurement. Fail closed when classifier down. claudetto deny rules and hard_deny stay in every tier. Source: B specs/low-hitl, C. have: plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/setup/settings.ts (autoMode); `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_MODE_MODEL` absent as intended.

#### Paid calls, decider, limits, setup
- [ ] **Paid runs bill the subscription.** `every paid tool in tools/: delete ANTHROPIC_API_KEY from child env; pass only CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN (2.1.325: present); error naming it if missing; init apiKeySource must be "none" or the arm check fails; no text tells the person to set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. - [ ] **Decider (Jev) rules.** `return pick only if probabilities[choice] >= 0.75 (or fallback names it) AND approval Noul answers no AND single-select AND pick names one option; else hand back to the model for the user; never threshold on confidence`. Confidence=(p_max-1/n)/(1-1/n) lets weaker choices through as options grow (0.75 at 2 options, 0.625 at 4; example 0.61/0.37/0.02/0.0 with confidence 0.48 handed back); approval P(yes) bound 0.2; multi-select handed back with no request. Single request builder (pinned jev-1.14.0, one try), then configured model (`deciderModel`, `deciderEffortLevel`, default claude-haiku-5-5/high, unlisted values fall back); temperature 0.3 yes/no, 0.2 approval only (set-wide 0.2 tightened every "no"); `decideCall` routes through `settle` so redaction and fallback apply; result scan reads each structured string as raw text (a field with `export API_TOKEN=` after a line break once reached the model unredacted, reproduced by failing test) and falls back to Haiku when the scan fails. Per-try timeout 12 s -> 5 s (slowest measured 1.8 s; worst case 39 s -> 18 s), keep 429/529 backoff, no retry on a missed deadline; set the wall-clock deadline per call site (`$.http.fetch` has an uncancellable 30 s cap, no `signal` on 2.1.318). Hook call site would add 152-262 ms warm per gated call and never `allow`. have: plugins/claudetto/hooks/kernel/decider/decider.ts, kernel/settings.ts (`deciderModel`). Sources: B decide, C, D.

Cited Hightouch: Jev alone 89.1% hard set, with escalation 95.3%; measured: 13.2% of claim-check Nouls in band, fallback matched 45 exactly vs 44 unsettled. supersedes C (decision 5). have: missing (`escalate` absent from plugins/). Sources: B decider, D.

The engine adds 2,048 to maxTokens and sends no `thinking` field, so thinking is on. have: partial (maxTokens in kernel/decider/decider.ts, features/setup/engine.ts). Source: D.

## Verification and Release

Notation: `L<n>` = input line numbers in theme-verify.txt. Verification tags: `2.1.325: present|absent` from cc-inspect `surface` on 2.1.325; `unverified` = no surface kind covers it or claim is third-party. Repo tags: `have|partial|missing` against `/Users/krystian/CodeProjects/xsyetopz/claudetto/plugins/` (grep, read-only). Basis labels kept (measured, cited, reported, inferred). No Reddit user named; owner statements paraphrased.

### Keep

#### Enforcement ladder and referee integrity
- [ ] **Rule ladder.** `for rule in rules: if mechanically_checkable -> hook|linter|CI gate; elif judgment -> short file, one-line reason ("because")`. Prose-only rules decay: rules that existed only as prose failed again on the same incident while lints, hooks and turn counters held (reported, session-log corpus); an explicit rule skipped ~1 in 3 (test-before-commit; n=21, prompt n=25,5, adopted). Repeated corrections: mine transcripts, promote each up the ladder (rule broken twice -> test/hook/lint/CI); after two failed corrections start fresh with a handoff; lint agent context files in CI; a rule logged 5 times (dead code) still recurred as written rule -> linter, deletion test (delete the line; tests stay green = nothing covers it), or independent reviewer. Reinject the 3-5 live corrections each turn (`UserPromptSubmit`; the "asisaid" pattern UserPromptSubmit+Stop+PreCompact+SessionStart, author claims ~50% shorter answers, reported). `InstructionsLoaded` logs which rule files loaded. Judgement clauses with no mechanism carry a reason. Rule hygiene extension: flag rules with no incident link or review date, propose converting recurring rules to hooks (L275). 2.1.325: present (hook-events InstructionsLoaded, UserPromptSubmit, Stop, PreCompact, SessionStart). Repo: partial (hooks/features/{repeat,protected,secrets}, tools/rules.test.ts). Sources: L3,L35,L44,L92,L275,L379,L63(I02).
- [ ] **Referee registry (user-owned values).** One registry of values the agent may not change alone: (a) bounds, limits, thresholds, budgets, caps, timeouts, retry counts and their files; (b) Checks: expected values, assertions, graders, eval cases, fixtures, baselines, CI and hook config; (c) model, effort, routing; (d) versions, release scope, public interface, dependency list; (e) project rules files; (f) settings, permissions, hooks, sandbox that govern the agent; (g) recorded decisions; (h) anything the user called theirs. Categories apply whether or not a file is listed; plugin product files (`agents/`, `hooks/`, `output-styles/`) are ordinary source. Mechanism: `PreToolUse` ask on any edit to a registry file, the ask names old and new value; deny approval claims with no user prompt behind them (project design D34, cited). `ask` only on files holding expectations and bounds (snapshots, limits), not all `tests/**`; user-scope autoMode soft_deny covers the rest. One registry serves tests, graders, bounds, baselines, hook files (extends I05/I14/I15). Repo: partial (features/protected, `protectedPaths` in plugin.json and setup/changes.ts; autoMode in setup/auto-mode.ts). 2.1.325: present (autoMode soft_deny/hard_deny used in setup, setting not queried separately: unverified for key names). Sources: L22,L33,L45,L52,L73(B2-10).
- [ ] **Make the referee unwritable.** Deny `Edit|Write` on `tests/**`, `.github/**`, hook files; managed-only hooks (`allowManagedHooksOnly`, `allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly` 2.1.325: present; `ConfigChange` hook 2.1.325: present); CI outside agent reach; the review-rules file read-only to the agent; any diff touching a test needs human eyes (CI diff lint, test changes without a label fail CI). Gap: `Edit`/`Write` hooks do not see Bash writes (a test loosened with `sed` passes an `Edit(tests/**)` rule) -> CI diff lint plus ask rules on Bash patterns; re-read the target after a rejected/interrupted write. Test-tamper detection has no sound structural gate: `.skip` match is swapped for comment-out, `if (false)` or a weakened `expect`; a changed expected value is indistinguishable from a fix by syntax; 100 commits delete a test file so ask-on-delete fires on routine refactors; no parser dependency (`typescript` absent, `Bun.Transpiler` exposes no tree, `ast-grep` is a process the lab cannot run); `bun test` has `--only`/`--bail` but no fail-on-skip; the measurable check is a post-run grader (supersedes nothing; C13.2 "not sound; no code" stands). Verify mechanism not output: agent weakening a test shows as `tests fail -> test file edited -> tests pass -> summary says green`; read the test diff before the code diff. Repo: partial (protected, `hooks/features/protected`); CI diff lint missing. Sources: L8,L61,L173,L195,L372,L73,L301.
- [ ] **Red/green regression test first.** Write a test that reproduces the bug and fails on pre-fix code, commit it red, then the green fix; count the bug fixed only when fails-before/passes-after; show the new test failing on unchanged code and state what should still fail; behaviour-preserving refactor starts with a characterization test passing on old code (~10 golden examples, run more than once) (n=31, n=6). Repo: partial (hooks/features/prompt/texts.ts:51 "write a failing test first"); commit-red step and characterization line missing. Sources: L84,L103,L264,L316,L413 (measured n=3: regression test 3/3 with Karpathy-style CLAUDE.md vs 0/3, Claude Code 2.1.312).
- [ ] **Tests assert behaviour, not prose.** Assert return values, error types, side effects, files, exit codes, tool traces; never wording, test names, backticks, keywords. A style rule becomes a test only when the user names that test; otherwise a lint or hook at the point of use (model-facing prompts, hook text). Pin error type and side effect, not message. Consequence: change-detector tests fail on harmless rewording, pass on wrong behaviour, push agents to edit prose until a regex passes. Removing a pin keeps the behaviour guarded (bare `toThrow()` must become a type/side-effect check, not nothing; over-correction removed a `.env` credentials guard). Prompt text that must stay is pinned by a manifest test that a mutant breaks (output style asks for the popup, one-message sentence, no `cheapSummaryEnabled` in plugin.json; L183) -- tension with the prose rule: only the named manifest pins, not a general wording lint. Reported cases: subagent-built backtick/word-list lint re-introduced prose pins (the owner ordered a wipe; pins found in six test families); the owner banned prefix-named test files and a subagent re-created them; fixture comment leaked the answer (both arms 1.00, without hint 0.47). Repo: have (rules/useTestPatterns.md:2, tools/rules.test.ts, tools/manifest.test.ts). Sources: L4,L9,L23,L24,L25,L50(S5.5,S5.6),L93,L183,L136,L82.
- [ ] **Never change a Check to pass.** S5.1: fix the code, never the Check (edit, delete, skip, disable, relax, widen tolerance, lower bound, swap baseline, `toThrow(".env")` -> `toThrow()`, mock out the thing under test); S5.2 general solutions, no special-casing inputs, hard-coded expectations, equality override, swallowed exceptions, exit-0 faking; S5.3 failing check: fix the cause or show evidence the check is wrong and ask; S5.9 fixtures, comments, prompts must not leak the answer; changing a case after seeing results is changing a Check. Held cases: lead refused a bound 190 -> 180 ("lowers a check"); red baseline left red until a clean `just sandbox` measure; grader fixes test-first with the defect reproduced on HEAD; consent-gated loosening kept a negative test. Enforcement: ask rules plus user-scope soft_deny (tree-changing git in shared tree; changing expected value/grader/eval case/baseline/limit unless the user's own message approves that exact change naming the value; killing by pattern; repeating a user-rejected action through another route). Repo: partial (agents/Heracles.md:11, auto-mode.ts). Sources: L8,L9,L10,L47,L50,L89.
- [ ] **Lock tests against the agent (commit-before-pass).** Commit tests before a subagent makes them pass; forbid edits to assertions/skips/deletions; `git diff` on test files; require a list of each test edit with old behaviour and reason. Spec/test staging flow: spec (living; missing acceptance criteria become explicit assumptions or open questions; discoveries update spec before they become a failing test) -> acceptance criteria -> failing tests staged `git add` -> implementation by a subagent that cannot edit tests -> separate no-context agent checks tests are not fake; ticket preflight collects every login/auth approval up front so an overnight run does not stall (reported, one thread). Repo: partial (Heracles.md:11); commit-before-pass not stated (low value, judged); staging flow missing. Sources: L89,L251,L336.
- [ ] **Validate graders and guards with known-good and known-bad inputs.** Every text-pattern grader ships >=1 known-good real-transcript answer it must pass and >=1 known-bad it must fail; prefer trace graders (tool ran, file changed, exit code) over wording; graders are regexes and file reads, not sentence judges (two careful judges agreed on 6 of 10); trigger the blocked action and confirm the guard fires; dry pass end to end (scaffold -> run -> grade) before the first paid run; symmetric decision rules, unit-tested on real intervals (reported asymmetric rule: resolve drop = loss when interval merely reached below -0.05, over-claim rise only when whole interval > +0.05; fixing reversed router decisions cost a $4.31 search); each bench grader rejects a known-bad run (empty patch, patch breaking a pass-to-pass test, transcript claiming tests pass with no run: `unprovenPass`). Repo: have (tools/guard-catch.test.ts, tools/bench/grade.test.ts, tools/claim-check.test.ts). Sources: L24,L27,L90,L130,L140,L195.
- [ ] **Eval suites must not silently test nothing.** Grader files without frontmatter load as zero graders (92 of 303 public cases never loaded on 2.1.295, reported): assert `grader_count > 0` per case, test a converted rubric against known-good and deliberately failing outputs; assert the skill actually loaded (session log shows the skill read; a colon in a description broke loading silently; a runner had disabled skills in both arms: 9.6 vs 9.6, with skill 10 vs 7, n=5); lint colons in descriptions (Metis.md description with `: ` that `Bun.YAML.parse` refuses; test reads frontmatter by line match as Claude Code does, measured). Repo: missing for grader-count/skill-load assertion; `plugins/claudetto/evals` exists. Sources: L214,L333,L403,L410.
- [ ] **Assert the arm and environment, not just the score.** For every probe/eval record: settings, plugins loaded, sandbox on/off, tools per arm, tool versions, and the model that actually served (`modelUsage` or capture `model`; `resolvedModel` and `modelsUsed` from each Agent result); fail the run on mismatch. Reported: every eval ran the plugin arm without settings profile; relative `--plugin-dir` made `just sandbox` load 0 plugins (all earlier sandbox checks tested vanilla); API key never passed into containers; a new case wrote its own log and flagged itself tampered; the sandbox dropped any `--model` not in `availableModels` and silently ran Sonnet 5.5 (earlier per-model Haiku/Fable/Mythos probes may be mislabelled). Eval profile: `CLAUDE_CODE_NO_MODEL_FALLBACK=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_REFUSAL_FALLBACK=1`, `switchModelsOnFlag:false` so substitution fails loudly (in 2.1.312 a bio/cyber-flagged request reruns on a fallback model and the session stays; gateways map `sonnet`/`haiku` aliases to older models); log `PostModelSwitch`, use `PreModelSwitch`. 2.1.325: present (env CLAUDE_CODE_NO_MODEL_FALLBACK, CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_REFUSAL_FALLBACK, setting switchModelsOnFlag, hook-events PreModelSwitch and PostModelSwitch). - [ ] **Bench discipline.** `run.ts` refuses a dirty `plugins/` or `tools/bench/`, records revision and `benchVersion` (sha256 of non-test tools/bench tree), faults, outsideFiles, copies transcripts; `report.ts` throws on mixed benchVersion, drops faulted runs, prints invalid runs and discriminating tasks; wrong arm plugins/model or a read of tools/bench faults the run; conduct rows: checkedAfterChange, lastCheckPassed, questionsToUser, opensWithFact, unprovenPass, seedNamed, outsideFiles, seedActedOn. Task schema: SWE-bench style, 9 merged bug-fix PRs (isort, zod x2, hono, black, pytest, attrs, sqlfluff, tox), issue text plus a seeded false teammate claim, PR tests hidden until grading, both arms `claude -p --setting-sources project`, same model/effort, `--permission-mode auto`. Grading hardening: `grade()` deletes `.venv`/`node_modules` and reruns install (a `uv sync` swapped pytest 9.2.0 to 9.1.1 and failed test ids); resets every test-path file the run changed; agent repo is a fresh `git init` fetching only base_commit and reachable tags (a full clone left the fix reachable via `git log --all --find-object` for 6 of 9); "acted on" matched by file set not substring; gold arm must resolve 9/9; each conduct row has a known-bad unit test; reread count uses overlapping line ranges with no change between; regrade from kept `model.patch`. Benchmark protocol (reported): lock harness, model, task, starting repo; change one variable; reset repo between runs; several runs; record wall clock, calls, tokens. 2.1.325: present (flags --setting-sources, --permission-mode). Repo: have (tools/bench/{run,grade,report,transcript}.ts). Sources: L130,L139,L140,L141,L308.
- [ ] **Controls for skill/rule evals.** Compare with no-skill baseline, same-length same-structure placebo (generic "write clean code" text), shuffled-sentence control, previous version; fresh sessions on one snapshot; judge by diff/test output; test on paraphrases (IFEval++ up to 61.8% drop under nuanced prompt change) and on each model used. Added text alone moves cost +2% to +16%; of 9 popular skills 2 beat placebo, 6 no better, 1 worse; none made runs measurably cheaper; caveman claimed -65% tokens, measured -2% output and +14% total cost (reported; 15 tasks, 30 runs per arm, Opus 5.5, Holm p=0.049). Record whether the skill was invoked (Skill tool calls); confound: a skill hook running the grader's lint hands the skill arm the answer key. Small-sample counterpart (reported): a ban-only writing skill 5/5 vs 0, a three-reviewer skill same score at ~14x cost, public skill tied 8/8; prefer short bans and facts the model cannot know over persona skills. Rule text pays tokens on every session: a ~650-900-token lean-rules plugin cut cost per task 51% (Opus 5.5 max), 55% (Sonnet 5.5 max), 11% (Opus medium), ~0 (Sonnet medium); a 3,100-token rival raised cost 11-30% at medium (measured by author, 18 tasks, promotional). Full "Everything Claude Code" install ~27k standing tokens (14% of 200k, reported). Repo: partial (budget/price-step features; tool-selection measure missing). Sources: L261,L334,L347,L348,L349,L165.
- [ ] **Done needs proof (completion gate).** Reject "done" on a summary: require pasted command output, exit code, test run or screenshot; rerun the command; orchestrator re-verification caught most subagent false reports; verify at the layer of the claim (UI needs a rendered page; eval harness a live run, not fakes: a fake-tested harness exited 0 on all-invalid runs); verdict from exit code and complete output, not piped through grep/tail/head; report denominator ("9 of 12"), measured vs inferred, the 2-3 least sure items; "fixed" only after rerunning the failing case else "changed, not yet verified"; leave a task unticked while a dependent check is red or a delegate reported partial; investigate a suspicious success (runtime far below normal, 100% pass/fail, baseline equals treatment); impossible or contradictory task: stop and report with evidence (success); a review round only when asked. Confirm fixes on the live artifact (curl of deployed URL, reloaded app, Playwright walk, real `claude -p`) not a simulator. Passing tests are not proof: also inspect invariants, boundaries, error paths, concurrency, resource lifetime, compatibility, caller expectations (R-AI-8). Repo: have (prompt/texts.ts:15 EVIDENCE, :86 FINISH, design.md:112; evals/done-needs-exit-code); completion gate TaskCompleted missing (2.1.325: present hook-event TaskCompleted).
