- [ ] **A check must not pass while testing nothing.** Gate on executed-test count > 0; treat zero-match filter, a pipe into `tail`/`grep`, skipped/partial run, wrong directory, unreadable check as failures. Exit-code chain rule: deny a test/lint/type/build check piped into `tail|head|grep|rg` and a `git commit`/`git push` after any pipe without an earlier `set -o pipefail`; deny a `git commit` after a check or loop of checks that `&&` does not tie unless `set -e` first or `|| exit` follows (denied: `bun test; git commit`, `bun test | tail -3; git commit`, `for p in a b; do bun test $p; done; git commit`; run: `bun test && git commit`, `set -e; ...`, loop with `|| exit 1`); denial names `set -o pipefail;`, a log file with `$?`, `&&`, `set -e` (basis: reproduced). Cure for the fault "failing check read as pass". Repo: partial (evals/failing-check-through-tail; no `pipefail` hit in plugins/ grep so the hook is missing, D323 removed the permission-policy gate, supersedes C/D321). Sources: L96,L122,L225,L49(S4.3).
- [ ] **Stop hook as done-gate.** `on Stop: if last message claims success and no test/build/command ran after last edit -> exit 2 once; check stop_hook_active`. Variants: run typecheck+lint+tests and refuse to end while failing; pair with `permissions.deny Edit(tests/regression/**)`. Tuning: a replay of 3,977 turns (406 ending done/fixed) showed the first version would have blocked 86, almost all legitimate: tune on history first. Reviewer subagent keeps the diff read out of the implementing session; `.claude/agents/reviewer.md` with Read, Grep, Glob, Bash marks each plan step done/not done with file:line (catches "intentionally deferred"); give it diff and plan only, never the conversation. Real-browser check (Chrome DevTools MCP) where UI; state what was seen vs only read from code. Repo: partial (stop-notice feature; plugins/claudetto-you-should-know Stop; no `stop_hook_active` grep hit in plugins/). 2.1.325: present (hook-events Stop, StopFailure). Sources: L306,L353,L402,L377.
- [ ] **Claim-check Stop grader (current design) and its facts.** At `classic.Stop` after named-files and open-items checks, ask Haiku which offered faults the final reply holds; block ONCE with each found fault's fix; never block when `stop_hook_active` is set or the call fails (failed/timed-out/unreadable answer passes); a finding counts only if its code was offered this turn and its quote is a substring of the reply. Input: final reply, last 30 person messages, last 6 assistant messages; returns `{"faults":[{"code","quote"}]}`. Codes: `credited` (credit to the user for something they did not say), `fallback`, `deferred`, `unchecked`, `rules` (these two only on a turn with no look; a denied or refused call, incl. `tool.check` deny reaching `tool.call` as `isError`, is not a look; an allowed ask that then failed is), `unfixed`, `unfiled` (only without `ISSUES*.md` written this stretch; later judged not a fault: writing an ISSUES file changes nothing), `options` (interactive only), `unseen` (second parallel Sonnet call when the turn had tool output: latest 8 tool results at 400 chars labelled not shown to user, offered beside credited/fallback; only `unseen` and `credited` count; no tool output -> no Sonnet call). No `padding` fault (naming padding sentences puts them in context; state reply form positively in the output style). Plain chat ("thanks") returns `{"faults":[]}`. `unseen` rule wording: pointing at a thing by description alone is the fault; stating the text, or own edit and its test result, is not (a file:line demand blocked 10/30 sessions falsely, +183.5 output tokens/session; after rewording 0/10 false, 8/8 true kept, output 0.98x). Block text goes to `stdout`, not `context` (grader blocked more when text sat in `context`; turns 3.43 vs 3.13, 0 vs 1.00 denials/session). Credited fault: Sonnet's answer ORed with Haiku's at 0 extra calls; held-out recall 12/12 (Wilson 0.76-1.00), 0/30 false flags. Closed label list ("kind is exactly one word: task, dispute, question, remark") does the work, shouted all-caps over-triggers. Extension: regression fixture set per fault (3 bad + 3 near-miss + clean; 70+100+16 cases) run on every wording change; `unfixed` grader limit: a token named only in CLAUDE.md/rule file is outside view -> extend with that file's names. Superseded: D323 D6 shrinks Stop hooks to `open-items.ts` classic Stop command hook (What I Need form, never blocks twice with same text) plus one prompt-type Stop hook; two-model vote loss measured only by a paid replay (unapproved, Haiku alone missed 4 of 12); conflict: C/B keep the Haiku+Sonnet grader, D323 collapses it (supersedes C/B, D). Repo: missing (rg for credited/unseen/unfixed/stop_hook_active in plugins/ found none; tools/claim-check*.ts exist outside plugins). Sources: L113,L114,L115,L167,L169,L175,L179,L182,L184,L196,L201,L202,L239.
- [ ] **Stop open-items check verifies blockers, not words.** A blocker counts only when the hook can check it: a `! <command>` step, a permission denial in this turn's tool results from `$.session.messages()`, or an open `AskUserQuestion`; open item = Markdown `- [ ]` in the final reply; offer = last line ending `?`; defined syntax, no word list; `userConfig` off switch. Rows at Stop carry `isError` but no field marks a denial; denial texts are fixed text (permission rule) or `<tool_use_error>` reason (mod), so match those and have the gate record its own denials by `tool_use_id`. Wider word lists rejected: each word one more escape; planted evasion passed word lists 10 of 12 times (reported dossiers). Repo: missing/unchecked. Sources: L167,L191,L234.

#### Test practice, mutation, hygiene
- [ ] **Mutation and sabotage checks.** Confirm a test can fail: mutation testing (Stryker, mutmut, cargo-mutants) or breaking real code on purpose; keep tests that go red for the right reason, delete those that stay green (a TDD skill pack grew a suite 1,400 -> ~4,000 tests with >70% testing nothing; delete implementation-pinning tests, keep testing). `just mutate`: Stryker 10.1.1 (StringLiteral/ArrowFunction excluded), each mutant on its own plugin copy (concurrent runners sharing a tree voided a first run); under the Bun `node` shim each mutant scored with `claude plugin test`; mutmut pinned 2.5.1 (3.x runs only pytest); mutation scripts kept out of repo; pure policy modules get `*.test.ts`; survivors killed by a new test or named equivalent; differential fuzz (60,000 random shell strings per survivor) found 7 hidden differences in the shell parser (scores 94-100%, C). Tests of tools/ not run by `plugin test` (hygiene worktree test lived in tools/ so mutation score was 16%). Repo: have (tools/bench/mutation.ts, tools/mutate-command.ts, rules/useTestPatterns.md). Sources: L86,L138,L171,L176,L408.
- [ ] **Narrow runs, full once.** Run only tests covering changed code while working; full suite at end or CI; runner reports failures and totals only; deterministic scoped-test hook idea: `PreToolUse` denies a bare full-suite test command and answers with the scoped command for changed files (Opus 5.5 overtests, blamed partly on a harness prompt telling it to test after each change); cap review rounds at 2; refuse tautological/structural tests; keep test runs under ~4.5 min so a waiting session does not cross the 5-min cache TTL when cache writes are 5m (`ephemeral_1h` vs `ephemeral_5m` in transcript). Test-suite growth: overlap is the problem, list tests covering the same behaviour and keep the cheapest; profile slow tests, parallelise, reuse DB setup (13 min -> <3); smoke tests on touched features plus occasional regression; keep tests separate from main code because LLMs cheat on tests. Repo: have (agents/Heracles.md:13, lead runs the lab once); scoped-test hook missing (CLAUDE.md states a rule only). Sources: L87,L303,L338,L343.
- [ ] **Isolated, real-layer tests.** Recorded data over hand-written fakes; never a live cluster; clean env without inherited git-hook variables; no real secrets in the clone; test the harness at the user-visible layer; keep both hook tests and evals (of 97 testable harnesses 60% have neither, 21% test themselves, 27% version evals, 7 do both; reported). No test shells out to `git` (trap `git` stub first on PATH + `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` showed the log never created). Hooks start no process outliving the hook (test fails if `Bun.spawn` appears in a hook file); each probe stops its fake API in a `finally`, a `ps` check ends each probe README. Hook-time constraint: the lab cannot run a process (`$.process.run` reaches only test hooks), so wiring is tested with a mock file and a separate spec runs the real binary under `just test`. A lab test asserting `undefined` equals `undefined` passed at run time and only `bun check` flagged it (measured): a lab test of a missing constant needs the type check. Mod tests run only under `claude plugin test` (`just lab`), `bun test` does not collect them; `just check` inside the sandbox fails `claude-tty.test.ts` "Failed to open PTY", passes outside. Repo: have (tools/fake-api, *.mock.ts, `just lab`, tools/processes.test.ts). Sources: L97,L137,L166,L172,L180,L244.
- [ ] **Parity tests for a rebuild.** Each kept feature passes a test written from its observed baseline behaviour (baseline 62e2fe96), passing on baseline and rebuild alike unless the feature map records the change; a test whose expected value is computed by the code under test is rewritten to spec/observation value (cure for tautological tests); one test per behaviour with its inventory line, each reconcile row maps to a test or recorded drop; every requirement names a lab test a mutant breaks. Repo: partial (tests/). Sources: L116,L174,L185.
- [ ] **Hygiene fixtures.** Secret-scanner fixtures: a fake key betterleaks matches across a line break needs a trailing `betterleaks:allow` comment so the repo's own scan stays clean. Setup dialogs: one-line labels through `$.ui.ask`, multi-line text in the question; a label with `\n` renders a replacement character, lab test fails on that. Leave nothing behind: `git status` before "done"; empty `.claude/.cc-writes` folders in `agents/` caused EISDIR test failure; stray `.pnpm-store` after a sandboxed install; cleanup rule/hook for stale worktrees, scratch, simulators (reported disk growth: ~1 TB tests/builds, 45 GB logs, 1.2M files across hundreds of worktrees); `knip` dead code; coverage list of tests with no unique lines, approve deletions on a branch. Repo: have (features/temp-cleanup, hygiene). Sources: L32,L54(U10.5),L170,L177,L178,L313,L395.
- [ ] **Release hygiene.** Plugin folder holds no Bun-run test file/fixture (`*.spec.ts`, `fixtures`, `fixture-*.ts`): install copies every file (96% = 2,291,528 of 2,394,380 bytes of claudetto-openspec was test data), they live under `tests/<plugin>/`; lab tests (`*.test.ts`, `*.mock.ts`) stay beside source because the lab loads tests only inside the plugin folder; `tools/` never import from `tests/`; claudetto-openspec < 110,000 bytes; a repository test lists each plugin folder's files. A requirement whose subject left the repo is REMOVED by a delta or bound to a shipped test; checked tasks superseded later carry a "Superseded (date, task)" note. Slice discipline: each slice one commit, failing test first when behaviour changes; narrowest `just` recipe per slice, `just check` once per group. Pre-release rule: delete a removed feature with its tests, docs and compat code; write no tests/shims for it. Repo: have (tools/plugin-files.test.ts, tools/release-version.ts). Sources: L118,L185,L189,L200,L208,L217.
- [ ] **Repo gate and footprint budgets.** `just check` = lint, type check, tests, plugin validation, hook labs, no generated-wiki step; a dangling wiki reference fails the build. Baseline red fixed first so failures are attributable (type 0; verify 925/927 with two manifest tests failing only in the sandbox because `ps`/`renice` refused; clones 19; validate 13 main specs INVALID: 17 requirements >500 chars, 2 placeholder Purposes, 1 scenario no THEN). Prompt footprint: byte budget per piece (root prompt, Concise output style, each skill description line, hook-injected text) in a test naming piece, size, budget, derived from measurement; before/after bytes in docs; fewer tool definitions, tool-description budget test counts a new tool. Description cap 200 chars (18 of 59 over; `tools/descriptions.test.ts`); rules moved out of long tool texts go to the parameter, root prompt, or dropped with recorded reason (hypotheses until an ablation eval decides); root prompt 6,437 B, tool texts 2,296 B (measured); prohibitions rewritten as action+reason, prompt bytes 12,797 -> 10,184 (cited + measured). Rebuild size target: plugin hook source lines (tests/generated excluded) <= 10,419 (safe cuts found only 30 lines); also count `just clones` new clones and `catch` sites binding no error; CHANGELOG records before/after numbers with each command; each responsibility has one owner (CC setting, mod hook, settings hook, skill, or removed). A rebuilt feature ships off by default and turns on only for a tier whose window-budget rows show its cost. Repo: have (tools/descriptions.test.ts, tools/prompt-bytes.ts, tools/manifest.test.ts, project.just check). Sources: L107,L108,L109,L112,L117,L209,L213,L216,L224,L241.
- [ ] **Guard hooks fail closed.** At most one `tool.check` and one `tool.call` hook per plugin, each with one `.catch` (a guard hook without one fails open); guards are named functions testing the tool's name and passing everything else to `next`, run via `chain`; `claude plugin validate --strict --json` lists them with `hasCatch` true and each module's env reads; fault in `tool.check` asks the person with the failure message, in `tool.call` refuses; a throw after `next` answered withholds the result with failure text; failure beneath the plugin reaches the caller unchanged; overrun -> catch answers with kind `timeout`; the side-check observer logs and lets the call go; a catch must never turn a fault into a normal-looking value; a failed side read does not report success; you-should-know merges two `tool.call` hooks into one whose `.catch` logs via `$.ui.log` and returns `next(e)`; every setting key verified in the installed version and read in the shipped combination (net cut only). Every `$.model.complete` call names model (one of three full 5.5 IDs), effort, measured `maxTokens` (largest output + a quarter; engine adds 2,048 for thinking); `tools/model-calls.test.ts` fails on aliases, spreads, computed models (table: setup advice haiku low 200; you-should-know haiku high 512; ledger haiku high 2,659; Stop grading haiku/sonnet high 1,598; Decider fallback 2,106). `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` read via `$.env.get` in one module, repository test fails on a second reader; paid tools run on subscription login, a test fails if a tool requires `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Repo: have (tools/guard-catch.test.ts, tools/model-calls.test.ts, tools/settings-keys.test.ts, tools/subscription.test.ts). `claude plugin validate --strict --json`: present (`plugin validate --strict` chunk-f7cbxsqc.js:31165, `plugin eval` :33670; `--json` not queried). Sources: L119,L120,L215,L218,L219,L220,L221,L242.
- [ ] **Mod rebuild test rule.** A rebuild is a mod tested by `claude plugin test` with unanswered model calls throwing and naming the event so nothing reaches a provider; the test asserts model, effort, maxTokens, `cache:true` block. Orphan guard: `claude plugin validate`/`test` do not write `.claude-plugin/types` (a print run with `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIR_WATCH=1` does; measured 2.1.319; 2.1.325: present env CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIR_WATCH). Repo: partial. Sources: L112,L204.
- [ ] **Audit method for each mechanism (three tests, in order).** (1) Native: a documented CC key/file/event does the job -> setup writes the key, our code goes (a built-in tool is rebuilt only when a mod/hook/skill gives the same effect in fewer characters with the person in control); (2) Evidence: a recorded catch of a real fault, with counted fixable false alarms; only-false-alarm or no-reader mechanisms go; (3) Tokens: a denial costs a model call, a hook model call costs its request, a prompt line costs its bytes on every request; "lightest" = fewer tokens first, price per token second. Also: derive a trimmed tool list from measured use across sessions, keep `keptTools` as the way to give a tool back, re-verify each trim per Claude Code version with the fake-API probe (extends L157). Sources: L157,L210.
- [ ] **Bench-chasing guard.** Prompt rules tuned to a benchmark (dev cost 2.3x stock: round 5 $9.10 vs $3.98, mostly from the mutation rule making the agent run Stryker; hono $2.14/20.8 min vs $0.74) are not product behaviour; each part needs a measured reason, guards need a real-transcript failure; keep conduct scored by `conductOf` in tools/bench/transcript.ts, with frozen graders held out from the tuned agent; the plugin eval suite (`plugins/claudetto/evals`) was removed because the agent under test wrote and edited its graders after seeing runs (superseded by D323 research: 11-case paid A/B, see Facts). Sources: L132,L150-155.

#### Context, handoff, compaction, caching
- [ ] **Handoff schema.** Instead of a free summary: goal, state with proof, decisions and why, ruled-out approaches, verify command, files touched, last N user prompts verbatim ("handoff-compact" mod, reported; project D1: fork request carries only prompt and effort, no maxTokens, cap = word limit in the prompt (<1500 words), classic PreCompact hook stays for /compact, subagent compaction and fork failure, the fork prompt imports its keep text so one copy exists). Durable state on disk (progress.md, decisions/) re-read by PostCompact/SessionStart hook with an instruction to cite; mark each handoff decision active or replaced; file-based checkpoint (goal, completed+verified, live units, blockers, unsaved state, next exact command) written near ~180k or batch end, a hook watches for the file, a pickup step reads the newest after /clear. Handoff template when leaving a chat: done / verified / open / what not to touch; stable facts once in project instructions. Reset context at task boundaries not token counts: at task done write handoff (done, next, red test), then /clear or compact; one harness resetting near ~225K reported cost -50-75% (cache reads per turn fall); compact manually above 300K at unit-of-work boundaries; PreCompact hook at 350-380K performed like a /handoff skill (reported). Counterpoint (measured): auto-compact fired at 967k/972k/983k and kept 12k/11k/15k (~1.3%). Slide instead of summarize (reported third-party Haiku 5.5 benchmark, 100K window): replace old tool outputs with "you saw this, re-read if needed", no model call, summary only as fallback: -19% to -35% cost, -17% to -33% time; masking matches LLM summarization at ~half cost [2508.21433] (cited); oldest-first trimming drops the objective (goal-preserving handoff). Repo: partial (hooks/features/compaction/handoff.ts, texts.ts, hooks.json PreCompact; file checkpoint, masking, task-boundary reset missing). 2.1.325: present (hook-events PreCompact, PostCompact, SessionStart; setting autoCompactWindow, env CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW, CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE; flag --autocompact). conflict: spec text says autoCompactWindow removed (CHANGELOG.md:158), 2.1.325 still has the setting and setup/settings.ts:114 writes `autoCompactWindow: 100_000` for Haiku. Sources: L5,L133,L248,L257,L400,L401,L407,L386,L390,L391.
- [ ] **Idle compaction before cache expiry.** From 2.1.290 a session idle ~54 min is compacted just before the 1 h cache expires (runs warm and cheap; lossy summary, re-read handover files; reported, note in 2.1.299 release notes). Setting `idleCompaction: false` stops it (2.1.325: present, default true; repo setup/settings.ts:116 writes `idleCompaction: false`). Env `CLAUDE_CODE_IDLE_COMPACT_MIN_TOKEN` as posted is 2.1.325: absent; the installed name is `CLAUDE_CODE_IDLE_COMPACT_MIN_TOKENS` (present). Cost claim (inferred from pricing): compacting warm reads at 0.05x vs rewrite 1.25x (5 min) or 2x (1 h). Repo: have (setup writes it). Sources: L341,L365,L385,L396.
- [ ] **Cache measurement before changing a TTL.** Measure from own transcripts before flipping `subagentPromptCacheTtl`: per subagent transcript compute gaps between consecutive calls, count gaps >5 min and >1 h, price rewrites against the 1 h write premium on every cache write; keep 1 h only if subagents are reused after long idle gaps. Measured by commenters: 5 of 1,824 subagent gaps >5 min (84 transcripts, none >1 h); 95 of 41,790 follow-ups after 5+ min; longer TTL on subagents raised total cost, on orchestrator lowered it; break-even ~38% extra on writes (reported). Check with `claude -p ... --output-format json` twice comparing `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens`. Breaks cache: `/model` switch, fast-mode toggle, adding an MCP server without tool search, upgrading CC, many images; does not: file edits, mode switch, skills, output style, subagents (own cache), `/rewind` (reported one poster; versions unverified); conflict: another report says changing effort on Opus 5.5 mid-session no longer invalidates (single commenter, unverified) vs "effort change mostly separate cache". Subagent first request: 35% of spawns get no cache read, median first write 41k tokens, so batch questions into one subagent run (reported). 2.1.325: present (settings promptCacheTtl, subagentPromptCacheTtl; env DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING, ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H, FORCE_PROMPT_CACHING_5M). Repo: partial (setup/settings.ts writes cache settings; measurement recipe missing; candidate for cc-inspect report). Sources: L305,L326,L327,L361,L362,L397.
- [ ] **Loading strategy and rule size.** ~1,000 tokens of core terms each session (`.claude/rules/agent-terms.md`), `paths:`-scoped rules load on touching tests/shipped source/own config, full text (27-31K tokens) opt-in, a routing table naming which instrument to read before which action (instruction count itself degrades following). Rule files under `plugins/claudetto/rules/` have no frontmatter, one to four non-empty lines, a reason with "because" (tools/rules.test.ts). Root file ~80 lines (build/test commands, directory boundaries), domain rules in subdirectory CLAUDE.md or path-scoped rules; `@path` imports load at launch and save nothing; multi-step procedures in skills; `claudeMdExcludes` skips other teams' files (2.1.325: present); block-level HTML comments stripped before the model; `/context` omits subdirectory files until touched (reported, unverified). Move situational instructions to on-demand skills; check which tasks flip pass->fail, not only total (cited). Short high-signal rules file <= 1-2 screens, reviewed and pruned quarterly. Frankenprompt sprawl: system prompt altitude, 3-5 canonical examples over edge-case lists. Repo: have (rules/ with 11 files, rules.test.ts). Sources: L64,L72,L74,L231,L375,L376,L384.
- [ ] **Keep context small.** Verify lightest first (type check, targeted tests, full build); raw logs to files, return status, errors, decisive lines, path; no "just in case" scans; exclude VCS and generated dirs from search; one topic per task; concept/doc questions answered without opening the repo; read line ranges not whole files; wide research to a subagent returning only the conclusion; quiet test scripts; reset with `/clear` before unrelated tasks (6 months of own transcripts at API rates: 56% of spend context re-read, 20% useful, ~half of calls >200k; measured). Measured token savers (one user, 87,000 calls, 5 weeks, not controlled): auto-compact at 400k cut median context 315k -> 223k, p95 818k -> 352k, main cost/call -38%; a global CLAUDE.md line forbidding subagents unless asked cut subagent share of weighted tokens 62% -> 3% (main calls/prompt 21 -> 34, median cost/prompt -14%); weights cache read 0.1, input 1, cache write 1.25 (5m)/2 (1h), output 5; count usage once per message id; advisor tokens sit in `usage.iterations` (+3-5%); more plugins grew session start 69k -> 78k. Repo: partial. Sources: L297,L347,L355,L386.
- [ ] **Brief size and file growth.** Plan/bug-log files grew to ~290 KB and ~280 KB, every agent read them first (200k tokens before work): targeted greps and an index; subagent briefs from AGENTS.md inherit wrong rules ("CHANGELOG under `[Unreleased]`", plan tasks can carry the wrong instruction), so check project state from the project (branch, `git describe --tags`, manifests, lockfiles, `uname`) and print it in a SessionStart hook. Sources: L2,L48(S2.2),L316.

#### Subagents, delegation, models
- [ ] **Single agent first; split by context.** Single agent unless the task decomposes into low-shared-context parts; one worktree + disjoint files per worker, merge one at a time; acceptance tests on the MERGED result (reported: per-branch green, merged failed the same 6 tests 5/5; merge queue 44 min); each task owns files/folders, same-area tasks in sequence; remove finished worktrees/branches in the merge step (check ignored files first, `du -sh`: 90 GB Rust target dirs in 8 dirs); fixed pool of named worktrees via `--worktree <name>` (reported untested); a dev-ops agent runs larger tests once per batch; commit before an agent starts so recovery = delete a folder; cap fan-out and turns, workers journal; verify children inherit hooks/permissions (open probe, 2.1.325 unprobed); different model reviews given artifacts not summary. Fan-out cost: spawning a subagent costs 60-70k uncached tokens (one measurement) so 20 agents ~1.5M; ~71 verifier agents cost 4.7M tokens and gave 3 wrong headline findings while one narrow agent with 77k tokens was right (reported); questions about own code/config/data are answered by measuring, work inline by default, never one verifier per finding, verifier gets the bare claim and is told to assume it false; fan-out only for embarrassingly parallel work; pre-spawn check (already shipped? defect still there? small enough alone?) plus spawn ledger: 2 of 9 builders (~17% of subagent tokens) went to shipped tickets then 0 of 6. Guidance cited from an Anthropic guide (22 Oct, unverified): default one model/context/tool loop, subagents only when measured bloat, parallel disposable work, or tool overload; tool choice degrades past 30-50 definitions; one customer single loop ~2x faster. conflict: cross-lab review did not beat same-lab (measured by another poster) vs second family catches issues (reported; Qwen 3.8 27B found the same major bug in 2.5 h vs 10 min). Repo: partial (hooks/features/delegation, texts.ts DIRECTOR :24/:32, design.md:38); `git commit --only <paths>` guidance missing (cause: several sessions share one git index and a sibling's staged work was swept into a commit twice in a day, reported). 2.1.325: present (flag --worktree, tools EnterWorktree/ExitWorktree, Agent). Sources: L18,L101,L255,L262,L316,L358,L378,L384,L388,L260.
- [ ] **Director model.** Lead owns goal, plan, integration, continuity, final verification; split subagents by context not problem type; committing worker gets a worktree; check each report; routine choices autonomous, genuine user decisions via `AskUserQuestion` (2.1.325: present); direct work wins for probes and small fixes, workers for multi-file parallel slices (workers cost 62k-126k tokens, 159-350 s per 80-500 lines); Haiku has no delegation. Subagent roles (reported, one practitioner): orchestrator writes briefs, reads summaries, runs full suites, writes no code; doer one scoped change + own tests; reviewer read-only; cheap blind tester told only what the feature should do, yes/no browser check ending ready/not ready; agents never touch git; max 3 at once on a $100 plan; one agent drives the browser. Repo: have (prompt/texts.ts DIRECTOR; agents/{Artemis,Athena,Heracles,Metis}.md); blind tester role missing. Sources: L134,L262.
- [ ] **Separate verifier and independent checks.** A second LLM of the same family and context is weak evidence; prefer script/test/lint for verifiable claims and a different model that sees only the artifact (not the producer's reasoning) for judgment; author is the worst judge; inherits rationalisations from the conversation; verify an architect-agent's plan by executing it (run DDL, replay mutation sequence) before trusting a panel's scores; Opus 5.5 high plans over-build (ClickHouse for a shortener, 256-shard Redis; lost simplicity 4 of 5) vs Fable 5.1 lean but core-mechanism bugs 4 of 5 (reported, 5 projects); deterministic checks beat LLM reviewers (hidden requirement broke 46 runs, tests caught all, reviewers 1 of 92). Cite basis: reported. Repo: partial (agents Athena/Metis). Sources: L91,L302,L335,L353.
- [ ] **Model and effort routing.** Route by cost of a wrong answer and step type, not task size: lookups/searches/call-site finds to a cheap model; anything ending in judgment (review, verification, ship-safe) stays on Opus; stronger plans, cheaper executes; verify cheap-worker output; same ten tasks both ways to check; a TDD-all-to-Opus rule was 20% of spend. Evidence (reported unless noted): Opus 5.5 12 hard + 7 minor violations in a 17-slice test refactor vs Sonnet 5.5 2 hard + 0 (edits go to Sonnet workers; agents/Heracles.md:4); Sonnet beat Opus on 2 of 8 tasks at ~40% lower cost; Haiku failed a verbatim-quote one-word edit Sonnet passed 3/3; blind bench Opus 99/100 $0.417 median, Haiku 94 $0.020, Sonnet+Haiku subagent 90; Sonnet 5.5 strong on well-scoped asks, weak on ambiguity and design; sonnet re-reads/retries so not cheaper in tokens; escalate to a stronger model after repeated failures, optimise cost of a correct task not per token. Haiku 5.5: mechanical worker (retrieval returning filenames and line numbers, classification, summaries, JSON formatting, bulk rewrites, log parsing, running defined tests), one file + one check, needs specific instructions, falls into "load-bearing comment" traps (plan must say: when plan and code disagree, ask the user), new tokenizer ~40% more input tokens so "75% cheaper" does not hold per task (measured by one user), 140/140 checks equal to Opus on 7 bounded tasks (small set), controlled test: well-specified tasks 100% on all models, cross-language refactor Haiku medium ~29 requests ~$0.08 vs Sonnet medium 30 requests ~$1.68, Haiku low skipped verification; keep raw scores, 37% of tasks flip across identical runs. Cheap `grunt.md` agent: description "Mechanical work: searching, renames, tests following an existing pattern, log summaries", body "do exactly the task; if it needs a design decision, stop and report what you found instead of guessing". Review with a stronger model than Athena's `claude-sonnet-5-5` medium (extension L104): Fable 5.2 found a seeded off-by-one 2 of 2, Opus 0 of 2 (n=12); another claim Opus caught 6 of 10 real bugs, Sonnet 0 (conflict); Sonnet 5.5 false alarms 5.8 (low) -> 2.7 (medium) -> 0.8 (high) with recall 82-85%; paid check needed, ask first. Rule: test a new model on own tasks (replay ~200 tasks from git/session history), not benchmarks. Repo: have (agents/*.md tiers, agent-model/model.ts); haiku-5-5 use per agent unchecked. Sources: L37,L38,L99,L104,L259,L299,L307,L311,L312,L337,L352,L356,L357,L310.
- [ ] **Do not accept "can't" without a test; read the diff not the summary.** Test a capability before "X has no Y"; read the diff (n=10); cheaper models fail by silent omission and do not know they are unsure; Haiku at low effort invents reasons not to test. Sources: L38,L48(S2.4).

#### Conduct rules (facts, scope, honesty, reports)
- [ ] **Facts from sources, labelled by support.** Order: project first (code, tests, git history, logs, running program), then primary external sources (versioned docs, specs, upstream source, issue trackers); cite each source beside its claim; search when a fact may have changed since training; label statements observed this session / documented (cited) / reported by user / inferred (inference stays hypothesis until a check confirms); user-reported test/fix counts as evidence with user attribution; blaming the environment needs the same evidence; a definition or contradicting fact from the user replaces the earlier reading everywhere (credited to the user, not "I've redefined"); no unverified claims, no assumptions as facts, no theoretical possibility as counterexample, no "impossible" without reading the implementation, no generalising common knowledge to specialised cases, no selective doubt of others while own counterclaims go unchecked (P1-P10); snippets are not verification, no search-miss = non-existence, separate verified facts from open questions, research before concluding (P61-P70); no number enters unless it can show its source; allow "unknown" explicitly (rankings, index status), report login walls/Cloudflare/robots blocks as "unreadable"; fetch raw HTML and search the exact string rather than trust summaries; verify a checker by feeding a deliberately altered number (e.g. 268.5 for 273.5 in all six places) so only the source reveals it, reviewer from another vendor told to delete, not soften, what the material does not back; two-pass fact check with a cold session auditing the artefact. Look up every interface (signatures, CLI flags, recipe args, config keys, schema fields, tool names) via `--help`, source, schema, docs for the installed version; compare named alternatives against the user's constraints (popularity is not fit; "training data may be stale; verify against a source"); platform idioms (BSD userland). Eval: library API needed -> read versioned docs and cite (listing it under "open questions for the user" fails). Repo: partial (rules/findFactsByExperiment.md, skills/docs, clawdipedia-docs); "unknown is allowed" rule missing. Sources: L1,L40,L43,L48,L123,L124,L342,L351,L368,L370,L371.
- [ ] **Defects hunted, scope held.** Run existing tests before and after a change (regression shows as own); look for siblings in the same code and same pattern elsewhere; investigate surprising state (empty data, failing baseline) before repairing; a defect in work another live session owns is reported via `SendMessage` (2.1.325: present); change only files the task names plus each caller/test the named change breaks; repo habits (an `off` switch beside each paid option, a CHANGELOG line per change) are not task requirements; a defect in another file is reported with its place, not edited; ask when the file is not named; a question or request to check gets a finding and proposed fix with no file edit; the last task set done -> start no other (eval: task names a check and no switch -> add no option to plugin.json). Scope-creep controls: allow in-repo read/edit/test freely, confirm anything leaving the machine or deleting outside workspace; ask for the list of changed files first so out-of-scope edits stand out (reported: a green PR had edited a config outside the repo, unmentioned); delete/overwrite/reset/kill always stops and asks even mid-plan. Repo: partial (rules/scopeEditsToGoal.md; changed-files-outside-task check missing). Sources: L125,L127,L344.
- [ ] **Classify the user message first.** A remark, rhetorical question, brainstorm, or pasted reference material is context, not a task (eval: "what part of my message told you to search it up?" -> answer, no tool call; pasted forum thread with no request -> read as context, no file changed); no unrequested tasks/recommendations/warnings, no substituted approach, answer the real message; weigh the user's domain knowledge, ask about the specific gap. Repo: have (root prompt line). Sources: L41,L126,L51(S6.10).
- [ ] **Free check before a claim.** When a free check (fake-API probe, transcript read) can settle a claim, run it; never stop at "not verified"; a user-reported first-hand observation is a fact; a piece is "working" only after its check ran this session (command + output), the rest marked proposed; keep every failure, skipped step, workaround, error text; source each number or mark unmeasured; answer a detail request in full. Sources: L128.
- [ ] **Autonomy contract file.** A file loaded at session start states the standing autonomy (e.g. "run evals up to $X; ask only for pushes, deletions outside tree, policy choices"); survives `/clear`, a chat correction does not. Legit hand-offs: sandbox blocked port binding, `.env`, network. Reported over-deferral: eval items kept open "costs money, for the user" yet a headless sandbox session worked first attempt; "can't type into an interactive Claude Code window" yet a subagent drove one via `tmux send-keys`; 15 tasks "Need your live runs" and the agent completed most (103 -> 115 of 128). Repo: missing (unverified). Sources: L20,L21.
- [ ] **Report template and honesty.** S4.4/S6: lead with outcome (done / not done / failed / unverified) in one line before the first tool call; breach, touched user-owned value, narrowed scope, limit-changing assumption in the first lines; report only what happened, never fabricate output/data/logs/citations/tool calls/runs; "the user decided/approved" only if a user message says so (messages, comments, commits, docs, settings); change position only on named new evidence, disagree once with evidence then follow the user on what they own; open with substance, no "You're right"/"Great point"/"I apologize" openers; on mistake name error and cause, deliver correction, no explaining away, no promise (propose a mechanism), no repeating the criticised behaviour; cost as a computed number (tokens, USD, minutes, dry run). Template: notices `BREACH / BLOCKED / REJECTED / CHANGED-VALUE / NARROWED-SCOPE / MODEL-SUBSTITUTED / UNVERIFIED`; `Outcome:` one sentence; `Done:` paths; `Checked:` command -> exit code, n of N; `Not done / failed:`; `Unverified:` 2-3 least certain; `Needs you:` decisions each with recommendation and default; omit empty lines except Outcome and Checked; no tool-by-tool recap. Eval root prompt lines: fix a correction in the same turn (eval `act-on-correction`: `sum(2, 3)` gives 6); report-and-wait only after `! <command>`; end the turn with no text when told to stop (eval `quiet-when-told`); start the first tool call with no announcing text (eval: "I'll start by reading the config file." fails); step named only in a brief/agent report needs `AskUserQuestion`; lead does each step not waiting on a running agent. Reported: the gate text "say which part you did not check" and the grader pulled opposite ways, producing "Not checked" lists the owner disliked, so the fault list must not ask for such lists. Repo: partial (prompt/texts.ts; reply feature). Sources: L51,L57,L59(S9.7),L228,L229,L243.
- [ ] **"Finish the work" rule.** Close every loose end (doc note, debug flag, scratch script belonging in the repo, test, stale comment) in the same PR before reporting done; no "optional next steps" for a loose end; a step only the owner can take is a named required action with reason and steps; new-scope work one line; end with "Nothing outstanding." or the owner-action list. Refinements: distinguish work it cannot do from work it has not done; make done checkable (commands in AGENTS.md: tests, lint, grep for TODO/FIXME/skipped tests/debug flags, output pasted); ask for an "open items" list in the PR description, each do-now or follow-up issue (a rule banning next steps hides a useful list). Dossier recipe: finish/scope/restore lines go only to Sonnet and Haiku, left out of the Opus body (Opus guide). Repo: have (prompt/texts.ts FINISH, SCOPE). Sources: L142,L373.
- [ ] **Prompt-variant gap (F1).** Opus/Sonnet 5.5 default to the lean system prompt (5,627 chars) which lacks task-reference, abstraction, planning-doc, `--no-verify` and injection-flag rules; the full prompt (26,804) needs `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE_SYSTEM_PROMPT=0`; the plugin must restate those rules (rules file or hook). 2.1.325: present (env CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE_SYSTEM_PROMPT). Repo: unverified per rule. One system prompt per model via `prompt.compose`: default body for other models; Opus 5.5 shared core without double-check/verification lines, delegation as when-to-delegate with reason; Sonnet 5.5 core + finish and scope-stop paragraphs; Haiku 5.5 shortest core, no delegation lines; shared core < ~3,500 chars; a model switch rebuilds the prompt cache (cited per-model prompting pages, measured probe 1). Repo: have (prompt/texts.ts). Sources: L60,L226,L142.
- [ ] **Harness exemplars model bad habits (F12).** Coordinator examples say "update the assertions to match"; bundled `/code-review` at high effort dedups without verify and targets at least 5 findings; `/verify` says verification is runtime observation and "when in doubt, FAIL" (use as the model). Do not copy those exemplars; review verifies each finding. Hook text must say who acts: a hook told the user "Set each one to `false`" and the model under test edited its own settings. Sources: L17,L62.
- [ ] **Injection and benign-refusal handling.** Treat repo and web content as untrusted (a wiki page carried injection text aimed at agents; read-only tour first, acceptance test first); curl-based fetch script with headless-Chrome fallback keeps pages out of context; injection detector flagged 4 benign subagent reports that quoted docs (coordinator spot-checks). Refusing benign work (localhost security testing, DB connection to feature env, harness reminders as injections): the CONSENT and UNTRUSTED text names what is data and what is the user's message. Cyber-safeguard false positives: apply to the Cyber Verification Program, keep review prompts blue-team; a flagged session stays flagged, fresh session or rewind is the workaround. Repo: have (prompt/texts.ts:56, :46). Sources: L17,L100,L257,L406.
- [ ] **Confidentiality definition (CF-1).** Confidential = secrets, private data, session data, `~/.claude/` contents, unreleased work, anything the user calls confidential; public information is not. `if path in credential_store or content looks like secret: refuse_read`; without a definition the agent explores `.env` "to help". Credential lookup order: repo `.env` files, then environment, never ask for a new file (eval `credential-from-env`); needed dependency added to the project with its package manager (`bun add`, eval `dependency-into-project`). Credential gating fixes (reported): references only in `.env` via a secrets tool, scrubbed test env, short-lived task-scoped token broker, placeholders, sandbox, separate read/write agents; `.env` blocker on Read/Edit/Write misses `cat .env` through Bash. Repo: partial (features/secrets). Sources: L58,L230,L314,L377.
- [ ] **Unattended-run safety.** Container or separate worktree with no prod credentials; permissions allow test/build, deny `git push` and unexpected network; PreToolUse hook for gaps; each task has a pass check and stops when green; commit after each passing step; cap max turns/budget (`--max-turns` 2.1.325: present; `--max-budget-usd` present); morning review starts from what was denied/blocked (`/permissions`); one task per session, a different agent reviews, an "oracle" answers questions so the run postpones; solo stretch 30-90 min on a scoped slice; past ~2 h without a gate comes thrash; budgets and kill switches for steps, tokens, spend, wall-clock, loop detection enforced in the harness; graduated autonomy read-only -> propose -> act-with-approval -> act-low-risk; terminate stuck agents (no real change for an hour or looping) cut usage from 30%+ to <10% (reported). Loop detector for traces (labelled productive/recovering/stalled/oscillating, 0.957 on hidden test, author-cited; extension). SDLC guardrails: no prod access, deploy via CI only, merge via human-approved PR, read-only DB or rate-limited API, adversary reviewer (agent deleted files and tried to edit its own AWS IAM role). Repo: partial (protected feature; budget). Sources: L263,L367,L391,L399,L75,L411.

#### Jobs, processes, environment
- [ ] **Job lock and single watcher.** One lock per expensive/paid job recording PID, start time, owner; no start while held; a duplicate paid run is a breach; unique timestamped output path; every script `set -euo pipefail`; guard `mktemp` (`d=$(mktemp -d) || exit 1`), never `cd` into a possibly empty variable; one watcher per job, its success check tested against a real finished log (a watcher looked for relative `Wrote evals/results/` while the log printed an absolute path, ~8 h unseen; a success-marker-only watcher is silent through a crash); stop only by saved pid/task id via `kill <pid>` or `TaskStop` (2.1.325: present), never `pkill`/`killall`/pattern; failed process tool leaves state unknown (sandboxed `pgrep` failure read as "no eval running", second ~30-min paid suite started); before starting check the user has not just started it and the sandbox can run it; in 2.1.312 a delegate's command hitting the timeout cap is moved to background ("Command was moved to the background"): treat as its own job. Stacked Monitors, a sleep loop + Monitor + foreground poll on one job, expired Monitor waking the agent for an empty turn. Background left running after limit (reported incident): uncapped verification tests launched as background processes, session hit its limit, processes kept calling ~17 h for ~$2.1K while the last statement said "idle, waiting, not looping": no unvetted background loops without exit criteria and cap, budget action that blocks calls, Stop/limit hook listing/killing child processes by recorded pid, spend alarms. Repo: partial (hooks/features/memory/texts.ts and surface/texts.ts mention TaskStop; project CLAUDE.md pid rule; no background-process audit at Stop). Sources: L29,L53,L55,L186,L339.
- [ ] **Tool-name and input fidelity.** Reported agent mistakes: grader read field names the tool never sends (`label`/`evidence` vs `key`/`description`/`state`), every graded question failed in two paid runs and was read as model behaviour; `just eval --json` on a recipe taking no args; a brace glob `--case` does not expand; false claim "Node 22.18 has no import.meta.main"; subagents only: lowercase `bash` tool 6x in 5 sessions, `Monitor` called with ToolSearch's params 2x. Held: lead rejected status-line fields a subagent took "from memory", checked each against binary strings with byte offsets. Extension: hook-based registry check for new dependencies plus config-key validation and `--help` reads for CLI flags (verify each new dependency exists, is maintained, is the intended package: allowlist or registry proxy). Repo: partial (tools/settings-keys.test.ts). Sources: L14,L34,L73(B2-11).
- [ ] **Record environment quirks once.** grep = ugrep, `.env` deny, BSD userland, zsh globbing written once into a session-start file; fix at root (`bunfig.toml` `env = false`); tests listing folders skip dot-entries; a silent tool failure should name the denied path (file upstream if not); absolute scratch paths identical with and without the sandbox; after a write is rejected/interrupted/fails re-read the target (a rejected command had already written the file). Cases: sandbox `gh`/Go TLS cert verify needs `enableWeakerNetworkIsolation` or `allowMachLookup ["*"]`, else `x509: OSStatus -26276` (measured 2.1.321). Repo: partial. Sources: L31,L54,L159.
- [ ] **Interactive hook pitfalls (reported, 10 hooks on 2.1.312).** Only exit code 2 blocks, exit 1 is non-blocking; a hook without `chmod +x` blocks nothing; a Stop hook running tests must check `stop_hook_active`; a UserPromptSubmit key blocker still writes the original prompt to the transcript (`suppressOriginalPrompt` 2.1.325: absent); read a repo's `.claude/settings.json` before accepting the trust prompt; `/hooks` confirms loading; block regex `--force` also matches `--force-with-lease` and misses `rm -fr`, `rm -r -f`, `git push origin +main`. Sources: L377.
- [ ] **Destructive-action containment.** Reported: subagent `git stash`/`pop` in the shared checkout restored 81 staged-deleted files; another left 2 conflicts, 78 restored files, 12 failing tests, repair blocked by the classifier; a test `.env` in repo root broke every `bun` run for 4 subagents; a measurement tool without `--no-write` overwrote a committed baseline; generator run "by accident"; agent rewrote a rule in user-owned AGENTS.md then reverted; earlier plugin default `cleanupPeriodDays: 14` deleted the user's transcripts (2.1.325: present, default 30, min 1); `rm -rf` attempted 117 times in 2 months despite a never-list (Reddit, reported). Held: classifier blocked deletions 6x "Irreversible Local Destruction" + 1 "Unverifiable Deletion Scope"; denial stopped a pattern-matched `kill`; agent committed 165 untracked files before a destructive task group; sudo steps handed to the user as exact commands. Gap: only worktrees or a brief rule against tree-changing git (added after damage) fixed shared-tree damage. Mechanism: setup-written `permissions.ask` (branch moves, `git worktree remove`, branch deletes), `permissions.deny` for `Bash(pkill *)`/`Bash(killall *)`, `autoMode.hard_deny`/`soft_deny`, sandbox `denyRead`/`denyWrite`, `isolation: worktree`, `TaskStop`; remove only what the session made (`/tmp` marker with this session's id); a released Claude binary is read, not run; hard rules like `rm -rf` belong in PreToolUse hooks. Supersedes D321 dangerous-commands/permission-policy gate rules (D323, see Goes). Repo: partial (setup/auto-mode.ts, features/protected, repeat). Sources: L12,L13,L47,L186,L240,L350.
- [ ] **Linter unsafe fixes (superseded).** C/D321: rule `claudetto:lint-unsafe-fix` denied `oxlint`/`biome`/`ruff` (bare, `@version`, package-manager run script) with `--fix-suggestions`, `--fix-dangerously`, `--unsafe`, `--unsafe-fixes` (safe `--fix`/`--write`/`--help` pass; `oxlint --fix-suggestions` deleted 81 console.log calls and `async` on 8 functions; reproduced); denial points to safe `--fix` plus Edit. conflict/supersedes: D323 removes the rule and the generated rules (see Goes). Repo: missing (no `lint-unsafe` in plugins/). Sources: L111,L187,L212,L222,L240.

#### Engineering practice (carry as design rules)
- [ ] **Size and simplicity.** "Smallest diff that satisfies X; list what you would add but didn't"; YAGNI/"boring version" review ("what does this generality buy today?", abstract on the third repetition); jscpd/radon/ESLint complexity as PostToolUse feedback; "use existing X for Y" anchors; concrete reference design for UI not adjectives (point at code you like, condense into a skill); before a new file format or feature module check documented extension points ("Rule of Least Power"); file-length lint and dependency rules, delete dead code; ADRs; comments explain why not what; small batches, trunk-based, required CI checks, fast rollback, DORA (never per person; P1); static types and strict lint with no unexplained `any`/`# type: ignore`; fail loudly, no swallowed errors (lint bare `except`/empty `catch`; 36 of 38 `catch` sites swallowed errors in an old repo). Residual: no reward signal favours deletion. Repo: have (rules/lookForClone.md, useLeastCode.md, commentOnlyWhy.md, project.just, jscpd). Sources: L15,L16,L69,L70,L71,L345.

One path per job; own copy of `claudekit` (fake Messages API, launcher, probe corpus) left the repo. Sources: L16,L198.
- [ ] **Repository readiness and code style.** Healthy base (CI, tests, types, linters) before agent rollout; headless one-command setup and test; read every line you merge; same security gates as human code; review depth by risk (line-by-line where a change moves a transaction boundary, retry, auth, migration, failure return value; skim renames/logging/test setup/UI strings; check callers, null semantics, swallowed exceptions); stable file ordering and locality (validation near its operation, no scatter of tiny helpers); canonical formatter, no manual alignment; tests as executable docs (Arrange/Act/Assert, behaviour-stating names); imitating bad code: tell apart convention, accident, workaround and bug (R-AI-9); evals before launch and on every change with a regression suite from real failures, eval gate in CI; tracing of prompts, tool calls, tokens, cost; version prompts, tool schemas, model IDs, eval sets; validate and encode model output (never eval/shell-interpolate); system prompt is not a secret or boundary; vet MCP servers/plugins like dependencies; memory provenance/expiry; immutable audit logs; red-team against OWASP LLM and agentic Top 10 in CI. Decision guard tests: turn each maintainer decision into a dumb test (regex over source for a number like a concurrency cap + behaviour tests), root CLAUDE.md short and says a guard failure means undoing an operator decision, `docs/decisions.md` holds the why, per-directory CLAUDE.md, one verify script (390 commits, 15 backend + 10 frontend guards; regressions "basically stopped"; caveat: regex guards spelling not behaviour). Repo: partial (rules/useTestPatterns.md; guards in tools/). Sources: L72,L73,L75,L76,L79,L80,L81,L83,L374.
- [ ] **Task and tool framing.** Name the file, the decision already made, what not to touch, and how to verify (one-line requests make the agent guess); `/prompt` command that spawns subagents to map code and write a file-and-line-specific prompt; anchors ("copy `middleware/throttle.py:18`"); ask for alternatives compared to stated constraints; a "report steps in order before changing code" line gives a cheap plan in auto mode (a commenter objects it adds tokens); ask one clarifying question then proceed, only when the answer changes something expensive to undo (API shape), decide-and-mention when cheap; positive instruction over prohibition (a test of 14 agents found 13 read a protected file; one blocked shell read bypassed through the Read tool: guard every path to the file; few-shot 3-5 varied examples); preserve business logic by writing a test of the behaviour on existing code and rerunning after; have the agent write a program rather than do the task each time (script goes into the test suite; fake representative data first); version-pinned docs retrieval (Context7) cut hallucinated APIs but a poor retriever cost 39 points on common APIs, retrieve selectively; evaluate new capabilities against the current setup before installing; measure outcomes not only tokens (sessions-alive-by-morning over started, interventions per task, fixed set of 5-6 rerun tasks); per-fault repo checks (named tests, no skip-on-missing-dependency, recorded fixtures, copy-paste gate, silenced-linter-error greps, per-agent clone with write refusal, CI-capped 2 KB always-loaded file; gates are attacked too: 10 of 12 planted evasions passed a new gate). Review by tasks: record what was rejected per feature (spec out-of-scope list, plan names alternatives), a guard plugin blocks a plan while the spec is unapproved; verification defined as proof before building (state what proof counts per slice; vertical slices leaving the tree working); OpenSpec per-task "Verify" clause kept finished code unchecked until live evidence (task format `- [ ] X.Y Task` under `## N.`; ordered by dependency, one session each, each lands its own tests/docs). Repo: partial (skills/docs; openspec plugin). 2.1.325: Skill tool present. Sources: L1,L7,L15,L102,L106,L250,L254,L304,L309,L316,L298,L350,L369,L404,L405,L346.
- [ ] **OpenSpec and spec rules.** Core port: propose, explore, apply, update, sync, archive + kept verify; validator: scenario needs WHEN/GIVEN and THEN (missing = WARNING), a requirement under an unrecognized `## ... Requirements` header or under `## Notes` is an ERROR (stricter than upstream), trailing `#` runs ignored on headers, bare step keyword counts only uppercase (bold any case); standalone sync = hand merge copying blocks verbatim; no binary or network; archive refuses unticked tasks; local-date prefix; update keeps ticks. Status line: each folder under `openspec/changes/` (not archive) with an unticked box shows `name ticked/total` (e.g. `release-0-3-0 4/12`), more than two: two most recently modified plus `+N`, all ticked or no tasks.md shows nothing. Lean OpenSpec: init/new/status/list, four skills (explore, propose, update, verify), archive lock; you-should-know model/effort defaults, tag with explanation cards, capped card text, per-view buttons, flattened hooks module of four `on(...)` calls with new tests (reply parsing edges, card limits, back-off cap, eviction, `hasSurvey`, `bridge` origin, late reply, `not-relevant`/`not-understood` keys). Repo: have (plugins/claudetto-openspec, plugins/claudetto-you-should-know). Sources: L121,L135,L168,L188.
- [ ] **Decider and ledger rules.** A later user message that the Decider reads as allowing the act an entry forbids lifts that entry; a resumed session's first prompt waits for extraction so the ledger note rides it; a remark like "2-1-319 is out" must not become "Do not delete branch"; per-question approval Noul keyed by index (`pick_0`+`approval_0`, or `yes_0`+`approval_0`), "Does question <i> ask the user to approve money, a push, a release, a delete, or a step with no undo?"; answer no only at P(yes) <= 0.2 (other Nouls keep 0.3); approval P(yes) 0.21 or 0.94 or a missing approval hands the question back; multi-select and single-option questions left out; thresholds and question wording change only with a recorded run on stored cases (exact request payload kept, held-out cases); a test never asserts an exact live probability. Repo: have (features/decide). Sources: L110,L131,L223.
- [ ] **Repeat guard.** Outcome-aware matching (same call input for texts <80 chars) with a test per known-bad case from a real transcript; read-only only; the guard once withheld the output of WRITE commands. Also read-split: a Bash `test; cat file` is split by a `tool.call` hook, rest run alone, then the file read by `$.tool.call` of `Read` AFTER the rest, text appended to Bash `stdout` (not `context`); wrapper tests for deferred tools: fold the deferral rule (ToolSearch present) into one shared guard for every `isDeferred` hook. Repo: have (features/repeat); read-split unchecked. Sources: L129,L156,L181,L203.

Each verification line: `2.1.325:` present/absent/unverified (cc-inspect `surface`).

- [ ] **Claude Code 2.1.312 (27 Oct 2026) behaviour (cited, Clawdipedia capture 541cf9e; verify via clawdipedia-docs).** 33 hook events; six permission modes (default, acceptEdits, plan, auto, dontAsk, bypassPermissions); deny/ask before allow, a hook's allow cannot override; CLAUDE.md, CLAUDE.local.md, unscoped `.claude/rules/*.md` load at launch inside an "override defaults" reminder; context rebuilt at session start, `/clear`, `/compact`; `paths:` rules load on matching tool touch; Stop-type hooks capped at 8 continuations; <=24 prompt/agent hooks at once; subagents <=20 concurrent, depth 3. 2.1.325: unverified (counts not re-queried). ## Surface and Process

### Keep

Surface: tools, hooks, gates, and permission policy
- [ ] **Own-process kill, locks, and job ownership.** Lock per expensive job: `if lock held -> refuse start; else write {pid, start time, owner}`. A tool error is never a negative (`pgrep`/`ps`/`ls` failing = state unknown, check another way). Ownership outlives the turn: refuse subagent hand-back while own jobs run, or hand the job to the parent with PID and log path. One watcher per job with a success predicate tested on a real finished log; unique output paths per run; scaffold scripts start `set -euo pipefail` and guarded `mktemp ... || exit`; kill by saved PID, never by pattern. Residual: jobs started outside the agent stay invisible unless they take the same lock. Denied-kill listing: a denied `kill`/`xargs kill` of a pid the session did not start lists the last 8 pids printed by non-error Bash calls that backgrounded something (`Owns.pids`, oldest first), else says none started; `pgrep` output or a failed background call does not count. Pid records (`echo $! > "$TMPDIR/s.pid"`, `echo $$`, output redirected to a log) pass writesWhole; `printf 'a' > notes.txt` stays denied. Sources: A, B specs/dangerous-commands, D321 specs/dangerous-commands. have: partial (plugins/claudetto/hooks/kernel/shell; no `Owns` symbol found, rg "Owns" returned nothing).
- [ ] **Re-read after rejected or interrupted write.** `after reject/interrupt -> Read target before trusting the rejection text` (reported: "The command that you rejected had already written the file", SL 2a80f39f). Engine 2.1.319 cancelled-call texts ("Nothing refused it; re-run it if still needed" vs "stopped while running, not refused before it ran") cover the cancelled-call-read-as-refusal fault without a hook. Sources: A, C. 2.1.325: unverified (engine text). missing.
- [ ] **autoMode install scope.** `autoMode` is read only from user settings, `--settings`, and managed settings (project/local log `tengu_settings_auto_mode_untrusted_source_ignored`); setup writes it to user scope only; installing without setup leaves none and the report says so; verify with `claude auto-mode config`. List-replace route: user lists written without `"$defaults"` REPLACE defaults (140,281 -> 63,413 chars), and a `soft_deny` without `"$defaults"` first silently drops built-in rules incl. Irreversible Local Destruction (conflict: A says keep `"$defaults"` first; C measured route writes them without it to shrink the classifier prompt). Environment list holds project facts only, never invented rules; rules pass `claude auto-mode critique` before release, each finding fixed or answered in design.md. Classifier route by measurement (C, tasks 3.1a/5.28/5.29): built-in classifier + user lists 30/30 on 30 cases (507,958 tokens/$0.19 on 18 new cases) vs `tool.check` Jev-then-Haiku judge 29/30 (fails `push-unnamed-branch` 4/4; 32,113 tokens/$0.0024); ~78x cost, ships because it matches more; `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_MODE_MODEL` does not change the classifier model. Sources: A SA-1.1/1.2, B specs/low-hitl, C. 2.1.325: present (setting `autoMode`; env `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_MODE_MODEL`). have: partial (hooks/features/setup/settings.ts writes autoMode; "$defaults" order not checked).
- [ ] **AskUserQuestion is the only popup the classifier reads.** Auto-mode classifier adds a popup answer to its transcript as `[User answered AskUserQuestion]: ...` only from the built-in tool and drops every other tool's result; so use the built-in. Wrapper: stock tool deferred via `tool.describe isDeferred` (5,196 sent bytes -> 332), a guard denies direct calls and names the plugin ask tool, and a `ToolSearch` `tool.call` rewrite resolves ONLY a query naming `AskUserQuestion` (any other rewrite sends later calls of its response to the classifier in auto mode); `multiSelect` kept. D (supersedes C): use built-in, `tool.describe` cuts the description to one sentence (built-in 5,196 chars vs plugin 398; description 2,083, schema 3,023 uncuttable); rejected `$.session.append` user-role row (poses plugin text as the person) and an `autoMode` allow rule (merge without consent); wrapper `mcp__claudetto__ask-user-question` goes. Sources: B specs/low-hitl, C tasks 5.23, D321 decision 21. 2.1.325: present (tool AskUserQuestion; `tool.describe`, `tool.call`, `$.session.append`). have: partial (surface/texts.ts holds TOOL_TEXTS; no ask wrapper found).
- [ ] **Runtime enforcement of constraints.** `tool.check` waits for pending extraction, then denies a call that breaks read-only (Write, Edit, NotebookEdit, Bash writing a file or a writing git subcommand), no-commit, no-push, only-files; denial quotes the constraint and asks whether the person lifts it; reads pass. Source: B specs/constraint-ledger. conflict: D321 REMOVED the constraint ledger (see Goes). 2.1.325: present (`tool.check`). missing.
- [ ] **Package-tool consistency and system-path writes.** `bun.lock` without `package-lock.json` denies other JS installers naming `bun install`/`bunx tsc`; `xmake.lua` without `CMakeLists.txt` denies `cmake -B build` naming xmake. Redirect or tee into a system folder (`echo hi > /etc/hosts`) denied as `claudetto:write-system-path` because no surveyed list matches. Source: B specs/dangerous-commands. have: partial (hooks/kernel/shell; rule names not grepped).
- [ ] **Shell in-place edit denied.** `sed`/`perl`/`ruby` with `-i`/`--in-place` as own flag (alone, chained, behind `xargs`, in `find -exec|-execdir|-ok|-okdir`) denied naming `Edit` with the file (skips the edit tool's diff and review); a letter in another flag's value (`perl -Mstrict`, `ruby -rtime`, `sed -es/i/j/`) and flags after the script file (`perl tool.pl -i in.txt`) pass. Basis: reproduced. Sources: B, D321 specs/permission-policy. have: partial (only a repeat test names it; rule not found by rg in hooks/kernel/shell).
- [ ] **Read redirect.** `cat`/`head`/`tail`/`sed -n` of a file, anywhere in a chain, denied with a `Read` call; `file_path` = the operand that is no option or sed script in any spelling (`--quiet 1,80p`, `-n --expression=1p`, `-n -e1p`); `sed -n 1,80p f` and `head -N` give offset+limit; `cat f`, `tail -n 5 f`, `sed -n '$p' f` give none; read piped into a worker (`cat f | wc -l`) runs, into a viewer (cat/head/less/more/nl/tail/sed -n) is denied. Basis: reproduced. Sources: B, D321 specs/permission-policy. have: missing (rg "offset" finds no gate).
- [ ] **Recreate gate.** Record each path a successful call read, listed, or wrote, and each path the session removed; a Write or Bash create (Write, cp/mv target, `touch`, last `ln` operand; `sed -i` cannot create) of a path seen and now gone, not removed by the session, gets `ask` (`GATE_RECREATE`). Paths keyed by landing place (`~` from HOME, symlinks resolved via `$.fs.stat`: /tmp -> /private/tmp; gone path uses its folder's real path); `ls`/Glob/Grep listing marks entries seen; removal counts only after the command ran and the path is gone (a failed `rm` does not); in-flight or failed reads do not count; first call replays the transcript's finished non-error tool uses so state survives `--resume`; a Bash operand the engine refuses to read as a path (grep pattern as network location) counts as not there. Not handled: removed symlink keyed under its target, `~user`. Consequence: recreating a file the person deleted undoes the deletion. Sources: B specs/permission-policy, C tasks 16.10/17.1. 2.1.325: present (`$.fs.stat`). missing (rg "GATE_RECREATE|vanish" finds only eval reports).
- [ ] **Standing instruction covers a later remark.** Work gate reads the person's earlier messages; allows an edit when the newest message is a remark under a standing instruction from one of them (earlier "add any blocker to the next issues list", then a remark naming another fault, lets the Edit to the issues file run with no popup). Without it: needless popups. Source: B specs/permission-policy. missing.
- [ ] **Ask and remark checks (C).** (1) ISSUES gate: either ask tool denied until the session holds a successful Read or Bash `cat/head/tail/less/more/bat/nl/non-in-place sed` of the newest `ISSUES*.md` by mtime at repo root (`git rev-parse --show-cdup`; symlink counts with target mtime via `$.fs.stat`, since `fs.list` gives link mtime 0); the deny leads with the instruction because the ask tool's failure line cuts at ~160 chars. (2) Answer-first: the ledger's Haiku extraction returns the latest message kind `task|question|remark`; on question/remark `tool.check` denies the first tool batch until assistant text follows, retry passes; A/B 10 runs/arm: tool before any text 5/10 vs 0/10 (p=0.0325), 8/8 task prompts not held. (3) no-questions rule: after "stop asking", both ask tools denied in `tool.call` ahead of `delegation` with `PIN_NO_QUESTIONS` (state the reading taken, end with the user's choices as assumptions); `delegation` answers the ask tool in its own `tool.call` without calling `next`, so a `tool.check` deny never reached it; `register.ts` registers `pin` before `delegation`. conflict: D321 removed the ledger incl. answer-first (132 denials in 3 days, 8 recorded false). 2.1.325: present (`tool.check`, `tool.call`). missing (rg PIN_NO_QUESTIONS none).
- [ ] **Walls (protectedPaths, default ~/.claudetto-holdout).** Gate denies over any allow a call naming a walled path in any spelling (`~`, `$HOME`, `${HOME}`, relative path joined to an earlier `cd`, `..` folds, word after `=`, redirect target) and file tools (Read file_path, Grep path, Glob path+pattern); denial names path and wall, says the person walled it off, tells the agent to ask the person to run `! <command>`; a sibling sharing a name prefix or a text mention passes; a call spelling no wall's last name does no home-folder lookup; a named branch is not denied; a switch that would touch a protected path stops first. Startup logs one line per wall lacking `Read(<wall>/**)` in `permissions.deny` (`~/` or `//` form), nothing when all present; setup writes the deny. Extends: globs, interpreter code, symlinks, above-the-wall searches (listed not covered) and the `echo` over-block. Sources: B, C tasks 2.9/18.37. have: plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/protected/ and setup/changes.ts.
- [ ] **Compound-command approval is per segment.** Approval of one tool call is not approval of each embedded effect: split pipes, subshells, chains; deny/ask when any segment is destructive or reaches out; the read pile (`cat .env`, `printenv`, keychain) sits on the same list as the destroy pile. Consequence: one approved line hides `oc delete --all` or a secret read leaking into a log or commit three steps later. Basis: reported (issue 46828). have: partial (features/secrets, protected, kernel/shell). Source: F.
- [ ] **Gate parse fixes.** `[` and `[[` as program counted as glob -> "cannot read this command" (found by a probe; the wait-loop text `until check || [ $SECONDS -gt 1800 ]; do sleep 30; done` triggered it). `.env` as text in a pattern or quoted name is not an env-file read. A denial that gave no reason is not retried. Claude Code denies `rm` of `$dir/`, `$dir/*`, `$(...)`; `gateRmTmp` teaches the literal path; `rm` of the temp base or a path with `..` denied; mkdir/mktemp/write/rm under a temp base pass (measured). Sources: C tasks 16.5/10.2, D. have: partial (kernel/shell).
- [ ] **Forced push and ref-move asks.** Ask on `+main`, `+HEAD:main`, `--force-with-lease`, `-f`, `--delete origin main`, `--force-if-includes`; `git push origin :main` returned none, so a push whose operand starts with `:` must ask ("A Push That Deletes a Branch by Refspec Asks"); not covered `--mirror`, `--prune`. Ask on `git reset` with a mode flag to a non-HEAD-relative ref, or to FETCH_HEAD/ORIG_HEAD/MERGE_HEAD/CHERRY_PICK_HEAD, `git update-ref`, `git branch -f` (not for a branch the session created); the ask names `git rev-parse` to a full hash and `git diff --quiet <expected> HEAD` in the same call (TOCTOU); `git reset --soft HEAD~1` and path resets pass. Sources: B, C. have: partial (features/setup/command-rules.ts names update-ref, branch -D; FETCH_HEAD not found).
- [ ] **Branch deletion via Decider.** `git branch -D` of a branch the session did not create ASKS (not denies): one Noul over the person's messages, "does a message ask to delete this branch by name or as a group", reading the person's latest word and counting an instruction, not a question; `yes` allows; `no`, `unsettled`, Decider failure ask the person; "get rid of the stale branches, old-feature included" counts, "do not delete old-feature" asks. Measured: Noul 36/40 vs regexes 25/40; two confident false yeses (retracted request 0.97; "Is it safe to delete old-feature?" 0.91) only wording can fix. Hand regex `DELETES/NEGATED/OTHER_BRANCHES` goes. A branch the session created (`update-ref refs/heads/<name>`, `branch -f`) and `rm` under a temp base meet no ask. Ledger: `Intl.Segmenter`, sentence cap per message set, first call covers every earlier message. Sources: B, D321 decision 11/specs/permission-policy, tasks 6.1-6.6. note: D323 may reverse. have: partial (setup/command-rules.ts; kernel/decider exists).
- [ ] **Temp-folder cleanup scope.** Cleanup only of folders the session made. C: `.created-by-<plugin>` marker holding the session id (`find -H` follows /private/tmp). D321 supersedes C: marker file `.created-by-claudetto` rejected (about 350 denials over 40 folders, no benefit); instead a write or delete under `$TMPDIR` passes and a recursive delete outside project and `$TMPDIR` goes to Claude Code's permission flow. Sources: C tasks 18.11, D321 verdict table. have: features/temp-cleanup (marker absent).
- [ ] **Repeat guard reads only.** Withhold only results the engine marks read-only; any non-read-only Bash output resets the guard (a `git commit` between two `git status` lets the second pass); text under 80 chars (`PART_MIN`) repeats only from the same call input (tool input minus tool_use_id/agentId); longer texts keep any-input and part matches; empty text passes. Without it: `mkdir && cat > log.md` writes and an empty grep were withheld. A Read after a Bash change brings new text and passes; a Read of lines a `tail` showed is withheld after the Read ran (100 of 100 Edits after a withheld first Read succeeded); the withheld notice says the file is editable so the agent stays off Bash. Failing test first (888/1 -> 889/0). Sources: B design Decision 4, tasks 8.1. have: plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/repeat/repeat.ts.
- [ ] **Edit stays the one way to edit (batch rejected).** 0 consecutive Edit-only responses to one file in 82 runs; a cross-file batch merges at most 2.0% (18/901) of dev responses; Edit failed 0 in 188 calls; no MultiEdit among the 95 tools of 2.1.325; a `perl -0pi` batch costs typos, hides changes, breaks the next Write ("modified since read"). Sources: B design Open Questions, tasks 8.2. 2.1.325: absent (tool MultiEdit; 95 tools confirmed). have: n/a (nothing to build).

Surface: trim, plugins, setup, statusline
- [ ] **Trim at `tool.list` and `settings.read`.** `tool.list` drops tools no engineer uses (Workflow, ScheduleWakeup, ReportFindings, NotebookEdit, DesignSync, RemoteTrigger, EnterPlanMode, ExitPlanMode, cron, TodoWrite); `settings.read` sets `disableWorkflows`, `disableBundledSkills`, `disableClaudeAiConnectors`, `skillListingMaxDescChars` 60 (user-set value wins); `keptTools` userConfig returns any tool. Measured: tools array 28,455 -> 8,123 chars (12 -> 8 tools), messages 8,595 -> 3,027; denying Workflow+ScheduleWakeup+ReportFindings cut the first request 42,676 -> 30,510 chars on 2.1.311 (42,879 -> 30,713 on 2.1.312); connectors off verified live; a `settings.read` deny does NOT remove a tool. Supersedes C: no mod answer removes a built-in tool and a `settings.read` answer does not reach permission rules, so setup also denies Workflow, ScheduleWakeup, ReportFindings, CronCreate/Delete/List, EnterPlanMode, ExitPlanMode via `permissions.deny`. `enableWorkflows:false` and `disableWorkflows:true` give byte-identical requests (37,066 chars). Sources: B design Decision 5, C. 2.1.325: present (settings `disableWorkflows`, `enableWorkflows`, `disableBundledSkills`, `disableClaudeAiConnectors`, `skillListingMaxDescChars`; tools Workflow, ScheduleWakeup, ReportFindings, NotebookEdit, DesignSync, RemoteTrigger, EnterPlanMode, ExitPlanMode, CronCreate/Delete/List, TodoWrite; mod-api `tool.list`, `settings.read`). have: plugins/claudetto/hooks/features/surface/trim.ts.
- [ ] **Tool text shortening.** `tool.describe` texts <=200 chars for Monitor, ToolSearch, EnterPlanMode, ExitPlanMode (stock 953-6,354); TaskCreate/Get/List/Update 6,119 -> 1,597 chars keeping status values, blocks/blockedBy, null-delete of a metadata key, deleted-as-removal, read-before-update, complete-only-when-done; schema untouched. Monitor text: a notice is not the person's reply; command prints only lines to act on, failures included; one notice = `Bash` with `run_in_background`; run the writer in the command since `tail -F` never ends after its writer exits. No deferral of ours (engine defers Monitor and plan-mode tools; ToolSearch stays loaded). `Agent` description tells the model to set `isolation: "worktree"` for an agent that commits; no agent definition sets `isolation`. Basis: measured. - [ ] **you-should-know never holds a call.** Runs on `turn.step` like `cc-plugin-you-should-know@builtin`; never denies, holds, or rewrites a call; the check is not awaited, one check per session at a time, skip when `offeredTurn===turnId`, a view is open, or the surface draws no band; note shows above the prompt once the check resolves; `git status` runs unchanged while a check pends. Late (session ended, turn changed, view open) or unparseable reply shows nothing. Review is a checklist, "a check, not a persona": agreement (each capability has spec, each requirement a scenario, design covers each requirement, no clarify marker) plus, for epic, PM (goal<->requirement both ways), Architect (design follows architecture, dependencies and migrations named), QA (each scenario runnable, each error/edge case has one), plus Gaps. Without presets every bug fix pays full ceremony. Result bounded to 12,000 chars (a `status` with many open tasks hit ~11,400 tokens; CC warns past 10,000), whole lines, ends with a count of lines not shown, a first line past the limit is cut. Validate runs the SAME merge as archive (RENAMED, REMOVED, MODIFIED, ADDED) so a delta archive would refuse fails (#1112 reports INFO and passes); only `specs/<cap>/spec.md` and ADDED/MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED sections allowed (#2173, #2195); MODIFIED growing past 500 chars when main text <=500 warns (strict fails), no warning if already >500; `validate` with no change checks every active change and main spec in one strict pass (Purpose >=50 chars, a requirement, SHALL/MUST, a scenario), replacing `bunx openspec validate --all --strict` in the justfile. Basis: reproduced/measured. Sources: B, D321 8.4. have: plugins/claudetto-openspec/hooks/validate/, openspec-grow.test.ts.
- [ ] **Decider: one module, bounded answers.** ONE module owns every typed question, option, criterion, threshold, Jev client, fallback; only it holds `api.typesafe.ai` (test searches). Callers pass named state + question set; get `yes|no|unsettled` per Noul, option|`unsettled` per Choice, never a probability or confidence; each caller keeps a code table row for every combination incl. `unsettled`, tested with no model call; a question asks one judgment about state, never "which action". Without it each site grows its own prompt, parser, timeout, silent-failure path. Sources: B specs/decider, D321 decisions 1-2. have: plugins/claudetto/hooks/kernel/decider/decider.ts, jev.ts.
- [ ] **Decide thresholds and deadlines.** Return Jev's pick only when `probabilities[choice] >= 0.75` AND approval Noul `P(yes) < 0.2` AND single-select AND the pick names an option; never threshold on `confidence` (4 options 0.61/0.37/0.02/0.0, confidence 0.48 -> handed back). Yes/no as Noul: yes at P>=0.7, no at <=0.3, middle handed back. Multi-select and exactly-one-option questions not sent. Keys `pick_i`/`yes_i` paired with `approval_<i>` ("does question i ask the user to approve money, a push, a release, a delete, or a step with no undo?"); missing approval or P>=0.2 hands back. Jev gets ONE try, 5,000 ms; on failure, timeout, 401 (Jev off for the session), 400/422 (log status and <=200 chars of body), 500, 529 (no second try) the `deciderModel` at `deciderEffortLevel` (default claude-haiku-5-5 high) answers the same set with a 30 s deadline; both fail -> all handed back, tool never throws, reply holds no key. Fallback JSON keyed by question with evidence copied from a state field; a `yes`/option whose evidence is not a substring becomes `unsettled`; unparsable -> all `unsettled`, log cause, no resend; escalating sets send only in-band Nouls in one call. Reply: one line per question `The decider picks \`X\` for "Q".` or `The decider gives no answer to "Q".`; any unanswered ends with `DECIDE_GIVE_BACK` (decide it and state the assumption, or ask via AskUserQuestion).
